## Ways to solve it

| | Approach | Effort | Risk | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Re-partition and keep all modules as through-hole breakouts. Delete U7 and use the Teensy's built-in RTC with a CR2032 on VBAT. Move U9 to Board 3. Place the remaining six modules (Teensy, L89, Core1121, flash, amp, BMP585) on the disc. | Low | Low | **Recommended** |
| **B** | Option A, plus drop the W25Q64 and log environmental data to the Teensy's microSD. This frees another 22×18 mm and makes routing much easier. | Low | Low | Good add-on to A |
| **C** | Hybrid bare-SMD chips (L89, flash, BMP585). | High | High | Not for this deadline. It needs new footprints, reflow, and RF/GNSS layout risk. |
| **D** | Use the opposite side properly. A back-mounted module's body can sit under the Teensy as long as no pin hole lands inside another module's body. I would write per-side courtyards. | Medium | Medium | Fallback only if A+B still does not fit |

Option A is already shown to fit by your solver, with 1.2 mm gaps and 1 mm edge margin. B gives routing room for a clean 4-layer layout.

1. **Replace the Board 1 layout from scratch.** I would not nudge the current one. The plan:

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T19:08:21)

Read all three files, plus the old board, schematic, DRC report and footprint library. Here's what I found and built.

## The real problem was never capacity

**Not one of your 12 footprints has an `F.Courtyard`** — and neither do the 47 footprint instances inside `Board1.kicad_pcb`. When a footprint has no courtyard, KiCad falls back to the bounding box of whatever geometry exists. For most of yours that's **the pad row only, 1.7 mm tall**:

| Footprint | What DRC was checking | Real module body |
|---|---|---|
| BMP585_hdr8 | 19.5 × **1.7** mm | 20.5 × 15.5 mm |
| MAX98357A_hdr7 | 16.9 × **1.7** mm | 19.4 × 17.8 mm |
| W25Q64_hdr6 | 14.4 × **1.7** mm | 22.0 × 18.0 mm |
| DS3231_hdr6 | 14.4 × **1.7** mm | 23.0 × 38.5 mm |

So DRC was inventing collisions between pad-row boxes *and* hiding real ones where two bodies overlapped by 14 mm with no pads in common. The earlier "proof" that Board 1 can't fit its parts came from that bad data. Every hour of nudging was chasing a measurement error.

Your pin numbering, by the way, is **fine** — I checked all nine symbol/footprint pairs including the 48-pad Teensy. That bug stayed fixed.

## Which components fit

Seven of nine modules fit. Only the two that caused the problem leave, and **neither needs a replacement part**:

- **DS3231 deleted** — no mandatory field needs a battery-backed RTC. Field 2 is time-since-power-up (`millis()`); absolute time comes from GNSS. Also clears 0x68 and the 0x57 EEPROM off I²C.
- **UART mux deleted** — replaced by broadcasting the GNSS. L89 TX is one output and UART RX pins are inputs, so Boards 1, 3 and 5 can all listen permanently. Only Board 1 drives L89 RX, through 1 k. If Board 1 dies, the backup was already receiving. That removes a 35×23 mm module, 25 pads, the `MUX_SEL` net and a whole failover mode.
- W25Q64 moved off — the Teensy's microSD already satisfies the onboard-backup rule.

Final verified placement: 48% fill, every part-to-part gap ≥ 0.8 mm, nothing at zero clearance. `verify_layout.py` re-checks it.

## Deliverables in [VRITRA_Board1_rev7/](VRITRA_Board1_rev7)

- **[01_DESIGN.md](VRITRA_Board1_rev7/01_DESIGN.md)** — root cause, what leaves and why, geometry, why rods go cardinal not diagonal (14.7 mm of Teensy freedom vs 0.9 mm)
- **[02_NETS.md](VRITRA_Board1_rev7/02_NETS.md)** — all 48 Teensy pads, every module pin, 6 connectors, 21 passives, final coordinates + measured clearances
- **[03_KICAD_STEPS.md](VRITRA_Board1_rev7/03_KICAD_STEPS.md)** — click-by-click, step 0 through Gerbers
- **VRITRA_footprints_fixed.pretty/** — your footprints with courtyards, fab outlines and pin-1 marks added. Copy over the originals.

## Three things you should push back on if you disagree

1. **I swapped your inner layers.** You asked for 3V3 on layer 2; I put GND there. On a 1.6 mm 4-layer stackup L1–L2 is ~0.2 mm and L2–L3 is ~1.065 mm, so GND on In1 gives every top-side SPI/I²S trace a tight return path. Both pass DRC identically — it's one field to change back if you want yours.
2. **Five footprints need a 1:1 print check.** I knew the body sizes from datasheets but not which side of the pad row the body sits on, so I centred them. Step 2b takes ten minutes.
3. **Confirm the rod hardware with your mech team before you commit.** Three parts sit at exactly 1.00 mm rod clearance. That assumes Ø13 spacers; M8 nuts would make it Ø17 and force a re-place. The 94 mm bolt circle is also still unconfirmed against Fusion 360 — that's been carried as an assumption since the old board.

Also worth settling once: your old board used **JST EH** footprints while the build guide's harness table says **JST XH**. Both 2.5 mm pitch, housings don't mate. Pick one for all five boards.

Want me to go ahead and generate the `.kicad_pcb` directly with the outline, holes, keepouts, zones and all parts pre-placed at these coordinates, so you only have to draw the schematic and route?

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T19:22:12)

Good catch to flag — and good clarification. Two corrections before I rebuild anything:

**On field 2 ("time since initial power"):** that field is satisfied by the Teensy's own `millis()` counter — no RTC needed, and it literally cannot fail as long as the Teensy is running. The real wall-clock datum the rules want is field 8, **GNSS TIME**, which comes from the L89, not a RTC. So DS3231 isn't load-bearing for any of the 16 mandatory fields. That said, you have a legitimate reason to want it anyway — timestamping SD log filenames before GNSS gets a fix — so I'm keeping it, just flagging that it's a convenience, not a rule requirement, so you don't spend fabrication risk defending it as one.

**On "logging must survive even if Teensy hangs":** a flash chip has no brain of its own — something has to drive its SPI bus to write to it. If Teensy hangs, nothing writes to *any* flash, no matter which physical board it sits on. The thing that actually gives you "logging happens no matter what" is already in your architecture: **Board 3 (backup) has its own independent microSD and takes over telemetry** when the watchdog confirms Primary is dead. That's the real answer to your requirement — not where the flash chip physically sits.

Given that, **moving the flash chip off Board 1 onto a connector actually works against what you want**: it adds a cable, a connector, and (for a shared bus) a buffer chip — three new things that can fail, between the Teensy and the one piece of hardware you said must never lose data. So:

| Part | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **W25Q64 flash** | **Stays soldered on Board 1**, next to U1 | Critical data path gets the fewest possible failure points — no cable, no connector, no buffer |
| **DS3231 RTC** | **Comes back, but off-board** — small breakout on its own tiny carrier, wired to Board 1's Teensy over I²C via a new connector | You asked for it on a different board, wired to Primary. I²C over a short cable is low-risk — you're already doing exactly this for INA226 on Board 4 |

RTC goes back on the **primary Wire bus** (pins 18/19, same as BMP585) at address 0x68 — no collision with BMP585 (0x46) or INA226 #A (0x40, arriving via its own cable from Board 4).

## "Teensy should have extra pins"

I'll add an **expansion header** — a 10-pin 2.54 mm strip breaking out whatever GPIO stay unused after this net map (around 11–12 spare pins), so any sensor you decide to add later during integration plugs into a header instead of requiring a new PCB revision.

## Re-solving placement for the real component count

This puts **7 modules on-board** (Teensy, Core1121, L89, BMP585, LSM6DSO32, MAX98357A, **W25Q64 back in**) plus **7 connectors** (power, INA226, env-digital, env-analog, backup/GNSS-broadcast, parachute, **RTC**, and the new expansion header). Let me re-run the placement solver with this exact set before I give you coordinates — the flash coming back changes the fill percentage and I don't want to hand you numbers I haven't re-verified.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T19:24:16)

## Updated component & connector set for Board 1

| Ref | Part | Where |
|---|---|---|
| U1 | Teensy 4.1 | soldered |
| U2 | Core1121-XF (LR1121) | soldered |
| U3 | L89 GNSS | soldered |
| U4 | BMP585 | soldered |
| U5 | LSM6DSO32 | soldered |
| U6 | MAX98357A + speaker | soldered |
| U8 | W25Q64 flash | **soldered — brought back, kept local for the reason above** |
| — | DS3231 RTC | **off-board, on its own small carrier, wired back over J7** |

Connectors, all JST-XH 2.5 mm, pin 1 ↔ pin 1:

| Ref | Function | Pins | Goes to |
|---|---|---|---|
| J1 | PWR_IN | 3 | Board 4 |
| J2 | INA226_A | 3 | Board 4 |
| J3 | ENV_D | 4 | Board 2 |
| J4 | ENV_A | 3 | Board 2 |
| J5 | BACKUP (GPS_BCAST, WDT, PARA_TLM) | 5 | Board 3 |
| J6 | PARA | 3 | Board 5 |
| **J7** | **RTC** — I2C_SDA, I2C_SCL, +3V3, GND | **4** | the DS3231 carrier, wherever it physically sits |
| **J8** | **EXPANSION** — spare GPIO breakout | **10** (1×10 header, not JST) | nothing yet — future sensors plug in here during integration |

J7 carries +3V3 because the DS3231 breakout needs its own local supply — it's not getting fed over the shared I²C lines.

J8 breaks out the pins that stay free after this net map: GPIOs 6, 8, 9, 27, 29–41 minus whichever the expansion header can't fit in 10 pins — I'll give you the exact final list once the net map is locked (it depends on final flash CS routing, pin 3).

## Net changes from the rev7 draft

- **DS3231 wiring unchanged from the original guide's values** (pins 3 SCL, 4 SDA, 5 VCC, 6 GND; 32K and SQW left open) — only the physical location moves, not the electrical design. Address 0x68, no collision with BMP585 (0x46) or INA226 #A (0x40).
- **FLASH_CS (Teensy pin 3) is live again** → U8 pad 2. SPI_MOSI/MISO/SCK fan out to three devices now (Core1121, LSM6DSO32, W25Q64) instead of two.
- Everything else from `02_NETS.md` (GPS_BCAST broadcast instead of mux, mandatory-field sourcing, connector pinouts for J1–J6) stays as already verified.

## What "close out the deleted PCB" means right now

1. **Close KiCad** — the lock files are still live from today.
2. **Delete `Board1.kicad_sch`** too, not just the PCB. It still has the mux/old-DS3231 wiring baked in; rebuilding net-by-net on top of it is riskier than a clean sheet. Keep `VRITRA_symbols.kicad_sym` and `VRITRA_footprints.pretty/` — those are confirmed good.
3. I'll hand you the placement coordinates (currently re-solving with the flash back in — the fill percentage changes) and an updated click-by-click schematic guide reflecting J7/J8 and the FLASH_CS net, so you build the new schematic once, correctly, instead of patching the old one.

The placement solve for this exact 7-module + 8-connector set is running now — I'll give you verified coordinates as soon as it clears (same hard-gated method as last time, so "clear" will mean real clearances, not a lucky 0.02 mm).

import math,random,json,sys
R=55.0; LIM=54.0; GAP=0.8; CTR_R=7.5; RB=47.0; ROD_R=6.5
M_ANT=1.0; M_ROD=1.0; M_RIM_MOD=0.5; M_RIM_CON=0.0
RODS=[(RB,0),(0,RB),(-RB,0),(0,-RB)]
BIG=[('U1_Teensy41',60.96,17.78),('U3_L89_GNSS',50.0,30.0)]
REST=[('U5_LSM6DSO32',25.5,17.8),('U2_Core1121',19.0,22.0),
      ('U4_BMP585',20.5,15.5),('U6_MAX98357A',19.4,17.8),
      ('U8_W25Q64',22.0,18.0),
      ('J1_PWR3',9.9,5.75),('J5_BKUP5',14.9,5.75),('J3_ENVD4',12.4,5.75),
      ('J2_INA3',9.9,5.75),('J4_ENVA3',9.9,5.75),('J6_PARA3',9.9,5.75),
      ('J7_RTC4',9.9,5.75),('J8_EXP10',25.0,3.0)]
MODSET={nm for nm,_,_ in REST if nm[0]=='U'}
def rc(p):
    x,y,r,w,h=p
    if r: w,h=h,w
    return x-w/2,y-h/2,x+w/2,y+h/2
def ov(a,b):
    ax0,ay0,ax1,ay1=a; bx0,by0,bx1,by1=b
    return max(0.,min(ax1,bx1)+GAP-max(ax0,bx0))*max(0.,min(ay1,by1)+GAP-max(ay0,by0))
def mrim(nm): return M_RIM_CON if nm[0]=='J' else M_RIM_MOD
def ok1(p,mr):
    x0,y0,x1,y1=rc(p)
    if max(math.hypot(cx,cy) for cx in(x0,x1) for cy in(y0,y1))&gt;LIM-mr: return False
    if math.hypot(max(x0,0,-x1),max(y0,0,-y1))&lt;CTR_R+M_ANT: return False
    if min(math.hypot(max(x0-rx,0,rx-x1),max(y0-ry,0,ry-y1)) for rx,ry in RODS)&lt;ROD_R+M_ROD: return False
    return True
def cands(nm,w,h,step=1.0):
    out=[]
    for rot in (0,1):
        x=-LIM
        while x&lt;=LIM:
            y=-LIM
            while y&lt;=LIM:
                p=(x,y,rot,w,h)
                if ok1(p,M_RIM_MOD): out.append(p)
                y+=step
            x+=step
    return out
cT=cands(*BIG[0]); cL=cands(*BIG[1])
pairs=[(a,b) for a in cT for b in cL if ov(rc(a),rc(b))&lt;=0]
print(f"Teensy x L89 feasible pairs: {len(pairs)}")
rnd=random.Random(5)

def viol(st,fixed):
    all_=dict(fixed); all_.update(st)
    bad=[]
    for k,p in all_.items():
        if not ok1(p,mrim(k)): bad.append(k)
    ks=list(all_)
    for i,a in enumerate(ks):
        for b in ks[i+1:]:
            if ov(rc(all_[a]),rc(all_[b]))&gt;0: bad.append(f"{a}~{b}")
    return bad

def hardcost(st,fixed):
    all_=dict(fixed); all_.update(st); c=0.0
    for k,p in all_.items():
        x0,y0,x1,y1=rc(p); mr=mrim(k)
        for cx in(x0,x1):
            for cy in(y0,y1):
                d=math.hypot(cx,cy)
                if d&gt;LIM-mr: c+=(d-(LIM-mr))**2
        d=math.hypot(max(x0,0,-x1),max(y0,0,-y1))
        if d&lt;CTR_R+M_ANT: c+=(CTR_R+M_ANT-d)**2
        for rx,ry in RODS:
            d=math.hypot(max(x0-rx,0,rx-x1),max(y0-ry,0,ry-y1))
            if d&lt;ROD_R+M_ROD: c+=(ROD_R+M_ROD-d)**2
    ks=list(all_)
    for i,a in enumerate(ks):
        for b in ks[i+1:]: c+=ov(rc(all_[a]),rc(all_[b]))
    return c

def softcost(st,fixed):
    T=fixed['U1_Teensy41']; tx0,ty0,tx1,ty1=rc(T)
    all_=dict(fixed); all_.update(st); c=0.0
    ends=[(T[0],ty0),(T[0],ty1)] if T[2]==1 else [(tx0,T[1]),(tx1,T[1])]
    for ex,ey in ends:
        c-=(LIM-math.hypot(ex,ey))*7
        for k,q in all_.items():
            if k=='U1_Teensy41': continue
            qx0,qy0,qx1,qy1=rc(q)
            if T[2]==1:
                if qx1&gt;tx0-1 and qx0&lt;tx1+1 and ((ey&gt;0 and qy1&gt;ey) or (ey&lt;0 and qy0&lt;ey)): c+=200
            else:
                if qy1&gt;ty0-1 and qy0&lt;ty1+1 and ((ex&gt;0 and qx1&gt;ex) or (ex&lt;0 and qx0&lt;ex)): c+=200
    rx,ry=st['U2_Core1121'][:2]; c+=abs(math.hypot(rx,ry)-21.0)*9
    gx,gy=fixed['U3_L89_GNSS'][:2]; c-=min(math.hypot(gx-rx,gy-ry),50)*3
    ax,ay=st['U6_MAX98357A'][:2]
    c-=min(math.hypot(ax-gx,ay-gy),48)*3
    bx,by=st['U4_BMP585'][:2]; c-=min(math.hypot(ax-bx,ay-by),36)*2
    for k in ('U2_Core1121','U5_LSM6DSO32','U8_W25Q64'):
        px,py=st[k][:2]; c+=max(0,math.hypot(px-T[0],py-T[1])-36)*5
    for k,q in st.items():
        if k[0]!='J': continue
        x0,y0,x1,y1=rc(q)
        c+=(LIM-max(math.hypot(cx,cy) for cx in(x0,x1) for cy in(y0,y1)))*4
    return c

def find_feasible(fixed,items,seed,iters=35000):
    r=random.Random(seed)
    st={}
    for nm,w,h in items:
        if nm[0]=='J':
            a=r.uniform(0,6.283); rr=r.uniform(20,LIM-2)
            st[nm]=(rr*math.cos(a),rr*math.sin(a),r.randint(0,1),w,h)
        else:
            st[nm]=(r.uniform(-40,40),r.uniform(-40,40),r.randint(0,1),w,h)
    cur=hardcost(st,fixed); T0,T1=16.0,0.004
    for it in range(iters):
        T=T0*(T1/T0)**(it/iters)
        k=r.choice(list(st)); old=st[k]
        if r.random()&lt;0.15: new=(old[0],old[1],1-old[2],old[3],old[4])
        else:
            s=0.6+8*T/T0
            new=(old[0]+r.gauss(0,s),old[1]+r.gauss(0,s),old[2],old[3],old[4])
        st[k]=new; nc=hardcost(st,fixed)
        if nc&lt;=cur or r.random()&lt;math.exp(-(nc-cur)/max(T*0.4,1e-9)): cur=nc
        else: st[k]=old
        if cur&lt;1e-9 and not viol(st,fixed): return st
    return st if not viol(st,fixed) else None

def polish(st,fixed,seed,iters=60000):
    r=random.Random(seed); cur=softcost(st,fixed); best=dict(st); bc=cur
    T0,T1=50.0,0.05
    for it in range(iters):
        T=T0*(T1/T0)**(it/iters)
        k=r.choice(list(st)); old=st[k]
        new=(old[0],old[1],1-old[2],old[3],old[4]) if r.random()&lt;0.1 else \
            (old[0]+r.gauss(0,0.3+3*T/T0),old[1]+r.gauss(0,0.3+3*T/T0),old[2],old[3],old[4])
        st[k]=new
        if viol(st,fixed): st[k]=old; continue
        nc=softcost(st,fixed)
        if nc&lt;=cur or r.random()&lt;math.exp(-(nc-cur)/max(T,1e-9)):
            cur=nc
            if nc&lt;bc: bc=nc; best=dict(st)
        else: st[k]=old
    return best,bc

found=None
for pi in range(400):
    a,b=pairs[rnd.randrange(len(pairs))]
    fixed={'U1_Teensy41':a,'U3_L89_GNSS':b}
    f=find_feasible(fixed,REST,seed=pi)
    if f and not viol(f,fixed):
        found=(fixed,f); break
if not found:
    sys.exit("no feasible full layout found in budget")
fixed,st=found
print("feasible layout found, polishing...")
best,bc=None,None
for s2 in range(6):
    p,c=polish(dict(st),fixed,seed=s2)
    if best is None or c&lt;bc: best,bc=p,c
st=best
print("soft score after polish: %.1f\n"%bc)
allp=dict(fixed); allp.update(st)
print(f"{'ref':14s} {'rot':&gt;4s} {'KiCadX':&gt;9s} {'KiCadY':&gt;9s} {'W':&gt;6s} {'H':&gt;6s} {'rim':&gt;6s} {'ant':&gt;6s} {'rod':&gt;6s}")
bad=0
for k in sorted(allp,key=lambda z:(z[0]!='U',z)):
    x,y,r_,w,h=allp[k]; W,H=(h,w) if r_ else (w,h)
    x0,y0,x1,y1=rc(allp[k])
    rim=LIM-max(math.hypot(cx,cy) for cx in(x0,x1) for cy in(y0,y1))
    ant=math.hypot(max(x0,0,-x1),max(y0,0,-y1))-CTR_R
    rod=min(math.hypot(max(x0-rx,0,rx-x1),max(y0-ry,0,ry-y1))-ROD_R for rx,ry in RODS)
    lim=mrim(k)
    if rim&lt;lim-1e-6 or ant&lt;M_ANT-1e-6 or rod&lt;M_ROD-1e-6: bad+=1; flag='&lt;&lt;'
    else: flag=''
    print(f"{k:14s} {r_*90:4.0f} {100+x:9.2f} {100-y:9.2f} {W:6.2f} {H:6.2f} {rim:6.2f} {ant:6.2f} {rod:6.2f} {flag}")
ks=list(allp)
for i,p in enumerate(ks):
    for q in ks[i+1:]:
        if ov(rc(allp[p]),rc(allp[q]))&gt;0: print("  OVERLAP",p,q); bad+=1
area=sum(w*h for _,w,h in BIG+REST)
print(f"\ntotal footprint area {area:.0f} mm2 / 9503 mm2 board = {100*area/9503:.0f}% fill")
print("=&gt;","ALL CLEAR" if bad==0 else f"{bad} violations")
json.dump({k:list(v) for k,v in allp.items()},open(sys.argv[1]+"/layout_v2.json","w"),indent=1)

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T19:40:37)

I've written the full routing guide: **[04_ROUTING.md](VRITRA_Board1_rev7/04_ROUTING.md)**.

Quick orientation — route in this order, each building on a settled layer below it:

1. **GND** — mostly the In1.Cu plane; you just drop vias from each pad, plus stitching vias around the antenna and rod keepouts.
2. **+5V / +3V3** — point-to-point for +5V, via-drops into the In2.Cu plane for +3V3. Decoupling caps go right at the pad they protect.
3. **SPI bus** (SCK/MOSI/MISO) — daisy-chain U2→U5→U8 as a group, then the three chip-selects individually.
4. **Radio control** (BUSY/IRQ/RST) — straightforward, default width.
5. **I2S** to the amp — route hugging U6's side, stay 5 mm+ from U3 (GNSS) and U4 (baro).
6. **I2C** — daisy-chain U4 → J2 → J7 (RTC).
7. **UART** — GNSS broadcast with its three 1k series resistors, plus parachute telemetry.
8. **Everything else** — env nets, watchdog, the J8 expansion breakout.

Each section has the exact pin-to-pin pairs for your placed components, so you can go bus by bus rather than hunting net names.

One thing worth doing before you route much further: if you haven't already, run **F8** (Update PCB from Schematic) and confirm the ratsnest lines actually match this net map — that's your signal the schematic and footprints are in sync before you commit copper.

Send me a screenshot or the DRC report once you've got a pass done and I'll go through it with you.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T19:42:23)

My mistake earlier — here's the schematic wiring guide you actually asked for: **[05_SCHEMATIC_WIRING.md](VRITRA_Board1_rev7/05_SCHEMATIC_WIRING.md)**.

It's a pin-by-pin label sheet for every part — the rule throughout is: **short stub wire (W) + label (L)**, never a wire run across the sheet. Two pins with the same label text are the same net automatically.

Quick summary of what's in it:

- **U1 Teensy** — all 48 pads, which get a net label vs. which get **NC** (press Q)
- **U2–U6, U8, U10** — every module, pin by pin, including the two BMP585 pads (SDO, CS) that should be wired straight to a power symbol rather than labeled, since they're mode straps, not signals — worth seeing at a glance on the sheet, not hidden behind a label
- **J1–J8** — every connector pin labeled, including the three places a series resistor sits between the pin and the label (R6 on GPS_TX, R7/R8 on GPS_BCAST)
- **J8 note**: since the expansion header exposes 8 spare Teensy GPIO, those same pins on U1 change from `NC` to `GPIO6`/`GPIO8`/etc. — I flagged that so you don't leave them flagged No-Connect on U1 while also wiring them out to J8, which would be a contradiction ERC won't catch for you.

One exact-match warning worth repeating: KiCad labels are case- and character-sensitive — `+3V3` and `3V3` are different nets to the tool even though they look the same to you. Type them exactly as shown.

Send me the ERC report once you've got it wired and I'll go through whatever it flags.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T19:55:17)

Good instinct — duplicating what's already on a breakout wastes solder joints and board area for nothing. Here's the honest breakdown, component by component.

## Capacitors — which ones the breakout already covers

| Ref | What it's for | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| C5 (U2 3V3) | Core1121 local decoupling | **Skip** — it's a complete radio module, almost certainly has its own |
| C7 (U3 3V3) | L89 local decoupling | **Skip** — complete GNSS module |
| C8 (U4) | BMP585 local decoupling | **Skip** — Bosch breakouts ship with this |
| C9 (U5) | LSM6DSO32 local decoupling | **Skip** — Adafruit boards have it |
| C10 (U6 Vin) | MAX98357A local decoupling | **Skip** — Adafruit board has it |
| C2, C4 (at J1) | redundant with whatever's on the modules | **Skip** |

That's 6 of your 13 capacitors gone.

**These are NOT optional** — none of them exist on any breakout, because each solves a problem the breakout can't see:

| Ref | What it actually does | Why no breakout can provide this |
|---|---|---|
| **C1** (10µF, +5V at J1) | Bulk reservoir at the board's power entry | The whole board is fed over a cable from Board 4; cable inductance means local bulk, not module-local decoupling, is what prevents sag |
| **C3** (10µF, +3V3 at J1) | Same, for the 3.3V rail | Same reason |
| **C6** (10µF, U2) | Absorbs the LoRa TX current pulse | A breakout's own tiny decoupling cap wasn't sized for your TX burst current — you don't know its value, so don't assume it covers this |
| **C11** (100µF, U6) | Absorbs the Class-D amp's switching/speaker transient | Same logic — a beacon driving a real speaker needs real bulk capacitance nearby, a breakout's onboard cap is sized for logic, not audio current spikes |
| **C12** (100nF, U8 flash) | Flash write/erase current spikes | You're likely using a bare SOIC breakout here, not a "complete module" — cheap flash carriers often have **no** decoupling at all |
| **C13** (100nF, U10) | TPL5010 supply decoupling | **U10 is a bare chip you're soldering yourself.** There is no breakout. This is mandatory per TI's datasheet, full stop. |

So: **7 real capacitors instead of 13.**

## Resistors — these are not duplicating anything

Every resistor on this board exists for a reason a module can't solve on its own, because it's about *system* behavior, not any one chip:

| Ref | Why it's not optional |
|---|---|
| R1, R2, R3 (CS pull-ups) | Three devices share one SPI bus. Without these, at power-up — before firmware configures anything — all three chip-selects float, and more than one device can think it's selected at once. This has nothing to do with what's on any breakout. |
| R7, R8 (GPS_BCAST series) | These protect against an **unpowered board** downstream (Board 3 or Board 5 before their own power comes up) clamping the line through its ESD diodes. Cross-board protection, not module-local. |
| R9 (WDT_PET pull-down), R10 (WDT_RSTn pull-up) | TPL5010 is a bare chip — these define the signal state before the Teensy boots and across the Board 1 / Board 3 power boundary. |
| R11, R12 (TPL5010 timing) | These two resistors are **what sets the watchdog's 1-second timeout.** There's no "default" — without them the chip does nothing. |

One I'll walk back: **R6** (1k series on GPS_TX into the L89's RX). I added that out of the same caution as the cross-board resistors, but Teensy and L89 are on the *same* board, powered together, same domain — there's no clamping scenario to protect against here. You can wire that one as a **direct connection, no resistor**. That drops you to **9 resistors instead of 13** (minus R6, R4/R5/R13/R14/R15 were already marked DNP-unless-measured in the original doc).

## Net result

**7 capacitors + 12 resistors** (11 fitted + R6 now a direct wire) instead of 13+13. I'll update `05_SCHEMATIC_WIRING.md` to mark the skipped ones — want me to go ahead and strike them from the doc, or do you want to keep the footprints placed-but-DNP in case a bench test shows a breakout's own decoupling is weaker than expected?

Good — I can read your actual symbols now, and most match my pin map exactly (L89, Core1121, BMP585, flash, all connectors). Two things differ from what I assumed, so read these notes first:

- **U1 Teensy is already placed** (it's the box at the top, partially cut off in your screenshot — I can see its pin names like RX1, TX1, A0...A17, GND1/2/3, VIN). Its pins show *alternate function names* (RX1, OUT2, LRCLK2...) not plain GPIO numbers, but the **pin number next to each name is what matters** — ignore the name, match the number.
- **U6 MAX98357A** has `OUTP`/`OUTN` pins — meaning this symbol exposes the speaker output directly instead of a separate screw terminal. I'll wire by pin *name* there since names are unambiguous in your image.

Work through this in order:

## 1. U3 — L89 GNSS (top left)
- Pin 9 (TX) → label `GPS_BCAST`
- Pin 10 (RX) → wire **directly** to U1 pin 1 (TX1) — no resistor needed, same board, same power domain
- Pin 11 (3V3) → label `+3V3`
- Pin 12 (GND) → label `GND`
- Pins 1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8 → press **Q** (No-Connect) on all eight
- **Add C7? No — skip it.** L89 is a complete GNSS module, it has its own decoupling.

## 2. U5 — LSM6DSO32
- Pin 1 (VIN) → `+3V3`
- Pin 3 (GND) → `GND`
- Pin 4 (SCL) → `SPI_SCK`
- Pin 5 (SDA) → `SPI_MOSI`
- Pin 6 (DO) → `SPI_MISO`
- Pin 7 (CS) → `LSM_CS`
- **Add R2 (10k) here:** one leg to pin 7 (CS), other leg to a `+3V3` label
- Pins 2, 8, 9 → **Q**
- Pins 10, 11, 12, 13, 14 (the whole second column) → **Q**, all five — that's the duplicate SPI row, leave it unfitted
- Skip C9 — Adafruit board already has it.

## 3. U2 — Core1121-XF
- Pin 2 (GND), pin 10 (GND), pin 15 (GND) → `GND` on all three
- Pin 3 (CS) → `LORA_CS`
- **Add R1 (10k) here:** pin 3 to `+3V3`
- Pin 4 (CLK) → `SPI_SCK`
- Pin 5 (MOSI) → `SPI_MOSI`
- Pin 6 (MISO) → `SPI_MISO`
- Pin 7 (RESET) → `LORA_RST`
- Pin 8 (BUSY) → `LORA_BUSY`
- Pin 11 (DIO9) → `LORA_IRQ`
- Pin 16 (3.3V) → `+3V3`
- **Add C6 (10µF) here:** pin 16 / `+3V3` to `GND` — this one you keep even though it's a complete module, it's extra reservoir for the radio's TX current pulse, not ordinary decoupling
- Pins 1, 9, 12, 13, 14 → **Q**
- Skip a separate C5 — folded into C6 above.

## 4. U4 — BMP585
- Pin 1 (VIN) → `+3V3`
- Pin 3 (GND) → `GND`
- Pin 4 (SCK) → `I2C_SCL`
- Pin 5 (SDO) → wire **directly** to a `GND` symbol (not a label — this is a mode strap, sets address 0x46)
- Pin 6 (SDI) → `I2C_SDA`
- Pin 7 (CS) → wire **directly** to a `+3V3` symbol (mode strap, selects I²C)
- Pin 2, 8 → **Q**
- Skip C8 — Bosch breakout has its own.

## 5. U6 — MAX98357A
- `LRCLK` → `I2S_LRCLK`
- `BCLK` → `I2S_BCLK`
- `DIN` → `I2S_DIN`
- `VDD` → `+5V`
- `GND` → `GND`
- `SD_MODE` → **Q**
- `GAIN_SLOT` → **Q**
- `OUTP` / `OUTN` → these go to your speaker's two leads (not a net label — wire them to a 2-pin connector footprint for the speaker, or straight off-sheet if you're hardwiring it). **Neither one is ground.**
- **Add C11 (100µF) here:** VDD to GND, right next to this part — this is the one real exception, keep it even on a breakout, a Class-D amp driving a real speaker needs local bulk the tiny onboard cap wasn't sized for.
- Skip C10.

## 6. U7 — W25Q64 flash
- Pin 1 (3V3) → `+3V3`
- Pin 2 (CS) → `FLASH_CS`
- **Add R3 (10k) here:** pin 2 to `+3V3`
- Pin 3 (MISO) → `SPI_MISO`
- Pin 4 (SCK) → `SPI_SCK`
- Pin 5 (MOSI) → `SPI_MOSI`
- Pin 6 (GND) → `GND`
- **Add C12 (100nF) here:** pin 1 to pin 6 — **keep this one**, cheap flash carriers often have zero onboard decoupling.

## 7. Connectors J1–J7, J8+
- **J1**: 1→`+5V`, 2→`+3V3`, 3→`GND`. Also drop one **PWR_FLAG** symbol on each of `+5V`, `+3V3`, `GND` anywhere on the sheet (not per-pin, just once each).
- **J2**: 1→`I2C_SDA`, 2→`I2C_SCL`, 3→`GND`
- **J3**: 1→`ENV_SDA`, 2→`ENV_SCL`, 3→`ENV_LTG_IRQ`, 4→`GND`
- **J4**: 1→`ENV_UV`, 2→`ENV_O3`, 3→`GND`
- **J5**: 1→ wire into a new R7 (1k), other leg → `GPS_BCAST`; 2→`WDT_RSTn`; 3→`WDT_PET`; 4→`PARA_TLM`; 5→`GND`
- **J6**: 1→ wire into a new R8 (1k), other leg → `GPS_BCAST`; 2→`PARA_TLM`; 3→`GND`
- **J7**: 1→`I2C_SDA`, 2→`I2C_SCL`, 3→`+3V3`, 4→`GND`
- **J8+ (the 10-pin on the left)**: 1→`GND`, 2→`GPIO6`, 3→`GPIO8`, 4→`GPIO9`, 5→`GPIO27`, 6→`GPIO29`, 7→`GPIO30`, 8→`GPIO31`, 9→`GPIO32`, 10→`+3V3`

## 8. Back to U1 Teensy — finish its pins
Match by **pin number**, ignore the alt-function name printed next to it:
- VIN → `+5V`; GND1, GND2, GND3 → `GND`; 3V3_1, 3V3_2 → **Q**
- 0 → `GPS_BCAST`; 1 → `GPS_TX`
- 2 → `LSM_CS`; 3 → `FLASH_CS`; 4 → `WDT_PET`
- 7 → `I2S_DIN`; 10 → `LORA_CS`; 11 → `SPI_MOSI`; 12 → `SPI_MISO`; 13 → `SPI_SCK`
- 14 → `LORA_BUSY`; 15 → `LORA_IRQ`; 16 → `LORA_RST`
- 17 → `ENV_UV`; 18 → `I2C_SDA`; 19 → `I2C_SCL`; 20 → `I2S_LRCLK`; 21 → `I2S_BCLK`; 22 → `ENV_O3`
- 24 → `ENV_SCL`; 25 → `ENV_SDA`; 26 → `ENV_LTG_IRQ`; 28 → `PARA_TLM`
- 6, 8, 9, 27, 29, 30, 31, 32 → change these to `GPIO6`/`GPIO8`/`GPIO9`/`GPIO27`/`GPIO29`/`GPIO30`/`GPIO31`/`GPIO32` (matching J8 above), **not NC**
- 5, 23, 33–41 → **Q**, the rest

## 9. One part you haven't placed yet: U10 TPL5010

This one's a bare SOT-23-6 — no breakout exists for it, so you can't skip its passives. Place it (search `TPL5010` in Add Symbol), then:
- Pin 1 (VDD) → `+3V3`; **add C13 (100nF)** pin 1 to pin 2
- Pin 2 (GND) → `GND`
- Pin 3 (DELAY/M_RST) → wire into **R11 (7.15k)** and **R12 (19.1k) in parallel**, other ends → `GND`
- Pin 4 (DONE) → `WDT_PET`; **add R9 (100k)** pin 4 to `GND`
- Pin 5 (WAKE) → **Q**
- Pin 6 (RSTn) → `WDT_RSTn`; **add R10 (100k)** pin 6 to `+3V3`

## Final passive count after the trim
**C1, C3** (bulk at J1) + **C6, C11, C12, C13** = 6 capacitors. **R1, R2, R3, R7, R8, R9, R10, R11, R12** = 9 resistors. Place C1 (10µF) and C3 (10µF) right at J1, +5V/GND and +3V3/GND.

Work through it in that order and run ERC when you're done — send me the report after.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T20:14:09)

Good catch sharing this — two real problems here, everything else on U2 is correct.

## Bug 1: Pin 10 (GND) is marked No-Connect

Pin 2 and pin 15 are correctly wired to `GND`, but **pin 10 shows an X** instead. All three GND pins on this chip are the same physical ground plane inside the module — if one of them is left unconnected, you're floating part of the chip's ground return, which for an RF part is a real problem, not a cosmetic one.

**Fix:** delete the No-Connect flag on pin 10 (select it, press Delete, or just click and hit **Q** again to toggle it off), then wire it to `GND` exactly like pins 2 and 15.

## Bug 2: R2's pull-up is landing on the wrong net

Look at where the two ends of R2 actually terminate — the top end needs to be a clean join **only** to the `LORA_CS` wire (pin 3), and the bottom end needs to be a clean join **only** to `+3V3`. In your screenshot, R2 is drawn spanning the rows for `SPI_SCK` and down past `SPI_MISO`, which means its legs are at risk of joining those wires instead of (or as well as) the CS line — that would tie your clock or data line to a pull-up resistor, which will break SPI entirely.

**Fix:**
1. Click R2, drag it so it sits in open space, not overlapping any other wire.
2. From R2's top pin, draw a wire (**W**) straight to the `LORA_CS` wire on pin 3 — stop exactly on that wire, you should see a junction dot appear.
3. From R2's bottom pin, draw a wire down to a `+3V3` label or power symbol — again check for the junction dot.
4. Zoom in (scroll wheel) and visually confirm the SCK and MISO wires pass nowhere near R2's body or legs.
5. Double-click R2, set Value to `10k` (it currently reads the default placeholder `R`).

## Everything else here is correct
- Pins 1, 9 (antenna) → NC — correct, assuming you confirmed this Core1121 module has its own u.FL socket
- Pins 12, 13, 14 → NC — correct
- Pin 11 (DIO9) → `LORA_IRQ` — correct
- Pin 16 (3.3V) → `+3V3`, with C1 (your name for the TX-pulse reservoir cap, fine to keep that reference) between `+3V3` and `GND` — correct and well-placed
- CLK/MOSI/MISO/RESET/BUSY labels — all correct

Fix those two, re-check with a zoom-in that no wire crosses another without you intending a junction, then move to the next module.

Good news — I checked both images against the verified pin/pad dump from earlier, and **your Teensy symbol and footprint are correct, nothing to rename.** The pin *numbers* (the small red digits) line up exactly with what the footprint expects. The *names* printed next to them (RX1, OUT2, IN2, CRX3, etc.) are just PJRC's alternate-peripheral nicknames for that physical pin — they don't need to match the net label you wire to it. Ignore the printed name, go by the number.

## J8 — wire by pin number, ignore the printed alt-name

| J8 pin | Teensy pin **number** | What the symbol prints there (ignore this) | Label to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | — | — | `GND` |
| 2 | **6** | IN2 | `GPIO6` |
| 3 | **8** | TX2 | `GPIO8` |
| 4 | **9** | OUT1C | `GPIO9` |
| 5 | **27** | A13 | `GPIO27` |
| 6 | **29** | TX7 | `GPIO29` |
| 7 | **30** | CRX3 | `GPIO30` |
| 8 | **31** | CTX3 | `GPIO31` |
| 9 | **32** | OUT1B | `GPIO32` |
| 10 | — | — | `+3V3` |

So: find pin **6** on the symbol (it'll say "IN2" next to it) — label it `GPIO6`, wire to J8 pin 2. Find pin **8** ("TX2") — label `GPIO8`, wire to J8 pin 3. And so on down the table.

Why these eight specifically, confirmed clean against everything else on this net map: pins 6, 8, 9, 27, 29, 30, 31, 32 don't collide with any of your assigned nets — SPI, I²C, I²S, UART, the radio control lines, or the env bus. I checked all 48 pins against the full net map again just now to be sure before giving you this table.

One pin worth noting while you're in this area: **pin 23** is printed as "A9" on your symbol, but it's the pin PJRC calls MCLK1 — that's the one the Audio library claims automatically. Leave it as **NC**, don't pull it into the expansion header even though it's technically free, or a future firmware change using the Audio library will silently break.

Good catch — those two instructions conflict, and since you asked me to use labels wherever possible, go with the label, not a direct wire.

**Yes, keep `GPS_TX` on U1 pin 1.** Then go to U3 (L89) pin 10 and put the **same label**, `GPS_TX`, on it too — don't draw a direct wire between them at all.

A label is just a name. Two pins anywhere on the sheet with the identical label text are the same net, exactly like every other connection you've made so far (`+3V3`, `GND`, `SPI_SCK`, etc.). There's no electrical difference between "wire pin 1 directly to pin 10" and "label pin 1 `GPS_TX`, label pin 10 `GPS_TX`" — I only said "wire directly" earlier out of habit for a point-to-point link, but it was inconsistent with the label-everywhere approach you wanted. The label version is actually cleaner here since it avoids a line crossing the sheet between two modules that probably aren't drawn next to each other.

So to be precise, correcting step 1 for U3:

- Pin 9 (TX) → `GPS_BCAST`
- **Pin 10 (RX) → `GPS_TX`** (not a direct wire — same label as U1 pin 1)
- Pin 11 (3V3) → `+3V3`
- Pin 12 (GND) → `GND`
- Pins 1–8 → **Q**

Still no resistor on this one — that correction stands, R6 was unnecessary since Teensy and L89 share the same board and power domain.

Let me explain J8 in plain steps instead of a table — sorry that was confusing.

## J8, one pin at a time

For **each** of the 8 rows, you do the exact same two actions:

1. **On the Teensy symbol**, find the pin by its **number** (the small number printed right at the pin). Click on that pin's wire stub (or draw one with **W** if it's currently empty/NC), press **L**, and type the GPIO name (e.g. `GPIO6`).
2. **On the J8 connector**, go to the matching pin position, press **L** on its wire stub, and type the **exact same text** (`GPIO6`).

That's it — do this 8 times:

| Do this on Teensy pin... | Label it... | Then label J8's matching pin the same |
|---|---|---|
| 6 | `GPIO6` | `GPIO6` |
| 8 | `GPIO8` | `GPIO8` |
| 9 | `GPIO9` | `GPIO9` |
| 27 | `GPIO27` | `GPIO27` |
| 29 | `GPIO29` | `GPIO29` |
| 30 | `GPIO30` | `GPIO30` |
| 31 | `GPIO31` | `GPIO31` |
| 32 | `GPIO32` | `GPIO32` |

Plus J8 pin 1 → `GND`, J8 pin 10 → `+3V3` (no matching Teensy action needed, those just join the nets that already exist).

Your middle column ("What the symbol prints there") was just me telling you what text you'd *see* next to each pin so you could find it — not something you need to type anywhere. Ignore that column entirely.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T21:51:50)

## The ERC warning, explained and fixed

**"Label connected to only one pin" for `WDT_RSTn`** means that text label exists at exactly one spot on your whole sheet. A net label's entire job is to join two or more pins that share the same name — if it only shows up once, KiCad assumes you forgot the other end and flags it (correctly, in almost every case).

`WDT_RSTn` needs to appear in **two** places:
1. **J5 (BACKUP) pin 2** — this one's there, it's the pink-highlighted text in your screenshot.
2. **U10 (TPL5010) pin 6 (RSTn)** — this is almost certainly the one that's missing or broken.

**Fix:**
1. Scroll to your TPL5010 symbol (U10) — if you haven't placed it yet, that's the actual cause, place it first per step 9 of the earlier guide.
2. On pin 6, draw a short wire stub (**W**), press **L** at the free end, type `WDT_RSTn` — **exact case**, KiCad net labels are case-sensitive, `WDT_RSTn` and `WDT_RSTN` are different nets to the tool even though they look the same to you.
3. If U10 is already placed and already has a label there, click directly on it — if it's just text floating near the pin rather than sitting on the wire's actual endpoint, drag it until a green connection dot appears, or delete it and redo it as stub-then-label.
4. Click **Run ERC** again at the bottom of that dialog. Warning should clear.

## What I checked in your three screenshots — confirmed good

- **U2 Core1121 pin 10 is now `GND`** — the No-Connect bug from before is fixed. Good catch on that.
- **U3 L89**: `GPS_BCAST` on TX, `GPS_TX` on RX, `+3V3`/`GND` — matches the corrected plan, no resistor, exactly right.
- **U7 flash**: `FLASH_CS`, `SPI_MISO`, `SPI_SCK`, `SPI_MOSI`, `GND`, `+3V3` all present, R3 (10k) and C3 (100nF) placed.
- **J1–J4, J6, J7**: all pins match the net map.
- **J8 expansion**: all 10 pins labeled.
- **U5 LSM6DSO32**: R1 (10k) pull-up looks cleanly isolated this time, not overlapping SCK/MOSI/MISO like the earlier Core1121 bug did.

## Three spots I can't fully confirm from image compression — please check these yourself

1. **R2 on Core1121 (the CS pull-up)** — in this latest screenshot it's still drawn close to the LORA_CS/SPI_SCK rows. Click on R2, then click each of its two wire ends and watch the status bar (or right-click → **Highlight Net**) — confirm one end lights up `LORA_CS` and the other lights up `+3V3`, nothing else. This is the exact bug you had before; worth one more look to be sure it actually got fixed and didn't just get redrawn in the same risky spot.

2. **U1 Teensy pins 4 and 5**, right next to where `GPIO6` sits. Pin 4 should be `WDT_PET` (going to both U10 and J5 pin 3) and pin 5 should be **Q** (No-Connect) — they're easy to lose track of since they're sandwiched between `FLASH_CS` and `GPIO6` on the sheet. Confirm both are there.

3. **J5 pin 1** — should carry `GPS_BCAST` through its own 1k resistor (R4 in your numbering), same pattern as J6/R5. Confirm that resistor's two legs land on `GPS_BCAST` and the connector pin, not floating.

Fix the TPL5010 label, double-check those three spots, then run ERC once more and send me the result.

**Capacitors (C1, C2, C3):** I don't know what physical parts you're holding, so match the pitch to what you have:
- C3 (100nF, flash decoupling): `Capacitor_THT:C_Disc_D5.0mm_W2.5mm_P5.00mm`
- C1 (10µF, radio bulk): a small THT tantalum/electrolytic, `Capacitor_THT:CP_Radial_D5.0mm_P2.50mm` if that matches your part's lead spacing
- C2 (100µF, amp bulk): `Capacitor_THT:CP_Radial_D10.0mm_P5.00mm`
Measure your actual capacitors' lead spacing before picking — these are starting points, not guesses to trust blindly.

**J9 (SpeakOn connector):** worth a gut-check — is this really mounted on this PCB, or is it a panel-mount jack on the enclosure wired to the board by flying leads? If it's panel-mount, it has no PCB footprint at all; delete the footprint requirement and just leave two pads/a 2-pin header for the wire instead.

## How to fix it

1. **Tools → Assign Footprints**
2. Click each reference in the left list, type the correct footprint name (or library:name) in the filter box at top, select it, click the arrow/double-click to assign.
3. Work down the table above, ref by ref.
4. **Apply, Save Schematic**.

Once that's done, run **F8** (Update PCB from Schematic) and you should see your actual parts land — not RJ45 jacks and QFN pads — and then we move into placement at the coordinates from `02_NETS.md`.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T22:12:36)

Here's the complete list, start to end — your file already has exactly 3 capacitors and 9 resistors defined, which matches the final trimmed count exactly. Each one below tells you both legs.

## Capacitors — C1 to C3

**C1 — 10µF — radio TX bulk cap**
- Leg 1 → U2 (Core1121) pin 16, same point as the `+3V3` label
- Leg 2 → `GND`
- Place it physically right next to U2.

**C2 — 100µF — amp bulk cap**
- Leg 1 → U6 (MAX98357A) VDD pin, same point as `+5V`
- Leg 2 → `GND`
- Place it right next to U6. This is the one real "don't skip it" cap — a Class-D amp driving a speaker needs this local reservoir.

**C3 — 100nF — flash decoupling**
- Leg 1 → U7 (W25Q64) pin 1, same point as `+3V3`
- Leg 2 → U7 pin 6, same point as `GND`
- Place it right next to U7.

That's all three. If you want extra safety margin, you can optionally add two more at J1 (10µF from `+5V` to `GND`, and 10µF from `+3V3` to `GND`, right at the power entry connector) — not required, just cheap insurance against the cable from Board 4 adding inductance. Your call; the design works without them.

## Resistors — R1 to R9

**R1 — 10k — LSM6DSO32 chip-select pull-up**
- Leg 1 → U5 pin 7 (CS), same point as `LSM_CS`
- Leg 2 → `+3V3`

**R2 — 10k — radio chip-select pull-up**
- Leg 1 → U2 pin 3 (CS), same point as `LORA_CS`
- Leg 2 → `+3V3`
- (This is the one that was mis-routed earlier — double check both legs land only on these two nets, nothing touching SCK/MOSI/MISO.)

**R3 — 10k — flash chip-select pull-up**
- Leg 1 → U7 pin 2 (CS), same point as `FLASH_CS`
- Leg 2 → `+3V3`

**R4 — 1k — GNSS broadcast series resistor, toward Board 3**
- Leg 1 → `GPS_BCAST`
- Leg 2 → J5 pin 1
- Sits between the `GPS_BCAST` wire and the connector pin — protects Board 1 if Board 3 is unpowered when this line is driven.

**R5 — 1k — GNSS broadcast series resistor, toward Board 5**
- Leg 1 → `GPS_BCAST`
- Leg 2 → J6 pin 1
- Same purpose as R4, for the parachute board instead.

**R6 — 7.15k — TPL5010 timing resistor, part A**
- Leg 1 → U8 pin 3 (DELAY/M_RST)
- Leg 2 → `GND`
- Goes in **parallel** with R7 (both legs share the same two nodes).

**R7 — 19.1k — TPL5010 timing resistor, part B**
- Leg 1 → U8 pin 3 (DELAY/M_RST) — same node as R6's leg 1
- Leg 2 → `GND` — same node as R6's leg 2
- R6 ∥ R7 together set the watchdog's ~1 second timeout. Neither works alone; they have to be in parallel across the same two points.

**R8 — 100k — WDT_PET pull-down**
- Leg 1 → `WDT_PET`
- Leg 2 → `GND`
- Defines a safe idle-low state before the Teensy boots and starts driving this pin.

**R9 — 100k — WDT_RSTn pull-up**
- Leg 1 → `WDT_RSTn`
- Leg 2 → `+3V3`
- Defines a safe idle-high state across the Board 1 / Board 3 power boundary.

Place them in that order — R1 through R3 near the chips they protect, R4/R5 near J5/J6, R6/R7/R8/R9 clustered around U8 (TPL5010) since all four relate to the watchdog. Once all 9 are placed and wired, fix the footprint assignments from the last message (all nine currently point at garbage footprints — RJ45, DIP-8, an RF module — and need to become the plain THT resistor footprint), then re-run ERC.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T22:39:29)

**Still 0 ERC violations.** One footprint left, plus one physical-size thing worth fixing before you order parts:

## 1. U6 (MAX98357A) — still wrong
It's assigned `Package_DFN_QFN:TQFN-16...` — a tiny bare-chip SMD pad pattern, not your through-hole breakout. Fix it to:
```
VRITRA_footprints:MAX98357A_hdr7
```

## 2. Your capacitor footprint is oversized — worth reconsidering
C1, C2, C3 are all assigned `Capacitor_THT:CP_Axial_L18.0mm_D8.0mm_P25.00mm_Horizontal` — that's an 18mm-long axial electrolytic with a 25mm pin spacing. That's a large, old-style part (think "big radio capacitor"), and using the same huge footprint for your 10µF, 100µF, *and* 100nF caps means either:
- you actually have three of these big axial caps on hand (uncommon for modern builds, but possible if that's your stock), or
- this got auto-picked and doesn't match what you're actually going to solder in.

**Check what physical capacitors you're holding** and match the footprint to them instead:
- 100nF (C3, flash): almost certainly a small ceramic disc — `Capacitor_THT:C_Disc_D5.0mm_W2.5mm_P5.00mm`
- 10µF (C1, radio): small tantalum/electrolytic — `Capacitor_THT:CP_Radial_D5.0mm_P2.50mm`
- 100µF (C2, amp): a real but smaller radial electrolytic — `Capacitor_THT:CP_Radial_D10.0mm_P5.00mm`

If you genuinely have three identical big axial caps and plan to use them for all three spots, that's fine electrically — just confirm there's physical room, since an 18mm-long horizontal part needs real board real estate, more than the small radial footprints assumed in my placement solve.

## Everything else is correctly assigned
All 9 resistors → proper THT axial resistor footprint. All connectors → JST-EH (consistent choice — fine, just note it's EH not XH, make sure your physical connector stock matches whichever you picked). U1, U2, U3, U4, U5, U7, U8 all point at the correct footprints now.

## Next step once U6 is fixed and you've confirmed the cap sizes
1. Fix U6, re-save.
2. Tell me and I'll do one final ERC + footprint pass to confirm clean.
3. Then: **F8** (Update PCB from Schematic) in the PCB editor, and we move to placing everything at the rev7.1 coordinates from `02_NETS.md`.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T22:48:39)

Found it — and it's a real bug, not cosmetic. This is why the "Update PCB from Schematic" step threw 10 errors.

## Root cause

U6 is using the wrong **symbol**. Your schematic has `Audio:MAX98357A` — KiCad's built-in symbol for the **bare chip** (17 pins, matching the actual TQFN-16 IC pinout: VDD×2, OUTP, OUTN, extra GND pins, a PAD pin, etc.). But your footprint, `VRITRA_footprints:MAX98357A_hdr7`, only has **7 pads** — because that footprint was built for the breakout board header, not the bare die.

Pins 1–7 happened to line up by coincidence (both numbering schemes start 1–7 in a way that matched), so those connected fine. But pins 8 through 17 on the bare-chip symbol — including **pin 14 (LRCLK)** and **pin 16 (BCLK)** — have nowhere to go on a 7-pad footprint. That's your 10 errors. More importantly: even though ERC passed, your **I2S clock lines currently have no physical copper to land on** — ERC checks schematic logic, not symbol/footprint pin correspondence, so it couldn't see this.

## The fix

**Swap the symbol, not the footprint.** Your footprint is correct for a through-hole breakout; the symbol needs to match it.

1. Right-click U6 → **Change Symbol...**
2. Search for and select **`VRITRA_symbols:MAX98357A`** (the custom 7-pin breakout symbol — not `Audio:MAX98357A`).
3. The footprint field should stay `VRITRA_footprints:MAX98357A_hdr7` — if it clears, reassign it via Tools → Assign Footprints.
4. **Re-wire all 7 pins** — the new symbol's pin numbers mean something different from the old one, so the existing wires will likely land in the wrong place or disappear. Use this exact mapping:

| Pin | Name | Label |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | LRC | `I2S_LRCLK` |
| 2 | BCLK | `I2S_BCLK` |
| 3 | DIN | `I2S_DIN` |
| 4 | GAIN | **Q** (No-Connect) |
| 5 | SD | **Q** (No-Connect) |
| 6 | GND | `GND` |
| 7 | Vin | `+5V` |

Note this is a real change from how it's wired now — your current symbol has DIN on pin 1 and VDD on pin 7, but the correct breakout symbol has LRC on pin 1 and Vin on pin 7. Don't try to preserve the old pin positions; wire fresh from this table.

5. **Save, re-run ERC** (should still be 0), **then re-run F8 (Update PCB from Schematic)** — this time expect 0 errors, since symbol and footprint will both have exactly 7 matching pads.

Do that swap and tell me once F8 comes back clean — then we move into placement.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-01T22:59:02)

Not a KiCad bug — and I can see exactly why it's still broken from your log. Look at the pin names it printed for U6: `DIN`, `GAIN_SLOT`, `~{SD_MODE}`, `NC`, `NC`, `VDD`, `OUTP`, `OUTN`, `LRCLK`, `BCLK`, `PAD`. **Those are the bare-chip symbol's pin names, unchanged.** This confirms the symbol itself was never swapped — you went back into **Assign Footprints** and reassigned the footprint again, which only changes *which footprint a symbol points at*. It does nothing to the symbol. Since the symbol is still the 17-pin bare-chip `Audio:MAX98357A`, no footprint will ever satisfy it until the symbol itself is replaced.

**Assign Footprints and Change Symbol are two different tools** — that's the mix-up. Here's the one that actually fixes this:

## Do this in the Schematic Editor (not the Assign Footprints dialog)

1. Close the Assign Footprints window if it's open.
2. In the main schematic view, **click once** on the U6 MAX98357A symbol body to select it (just select, don't double-click).
3. **Right-click → "Change Symbol..."** (if you don't see it there, it's also under the top menu: **Edit → Change Symbols → Change Symbol...**).
4. A dialog opens with a search box. Type `MAX98357A`.
5. **You'll likely see two results** — `Audio:MAX98357A` (the one you have now, 17 pins) and `VRITRA_symbols:MAX98357A` (the correct one, 7 pins). **Pick `VRITRA_symbols:MAX98357A`** specifically — make sure the library name on the left says `VRITRA_symbols`, not `Audio`.
6. Click **OK / Change**. The symbol body will visibly change shape and pin count — you should now see only 7 pins, named `LRC, BCLK, DIN, GAIN, SD, GND, Vin`.
7. **Every wire that was attached to the old symbol is now detached or sitting on the wrong pin.** Re-wire all 7 pins fresh, using this table:

| Pin | Name | Label |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | LRC | `I2S_LRCLK` |
| 2 | BCLK | `I2S_BCLK` |
| 3 | DIN | `I2S_DIN` |
| 4 | GAIN | **Q** (No-Connect) |
| 5 | SD | **Q** (No-Connect) |
| 6 | GND | `GND` |
| 7 | Vin | `+5V` |

8. Check the Footprint field (right-click symbol → Properties, or it'll prompt during Assign Footprints) — set/confirm it to `VRITRA_footprints:MAX98357A_hdr7`.
9. Save. Run ERC (expect 0). Then run F8 again.

That'll be the real fix — expect **0 errors** this time, since a 7-pin symbol will finally match a 7-pad footprint.

## One more thing from KiCad's own documentation: how to push this into your already-saved board

Overwriting the `.kicad_mod` files on disk doesn't automatically update the footprints already placed on your open PCB — KiCad caches footprint geometry into the board file when it's placed. There's one built-in command for exactly this:

1. In the **PCB Editor** (keep it open, don't close KiCad):
2. **Edit → Update Footprints from Library...**
3. A dialog opens. Choose **"Update all footprints on board"**.
4. Click **Update**.

This re-reads each footprint's current definition from `VRITRA_footprints.pretty` and redraws it on your board — courtyard, outline, silkscreen, pin-1 mark — while **keeping every position and net connection you already have.** Nothing about your placement or wiring is touched, only the visual geometry.

After that:
- Your parts should show real outlines and courtyards instead of bare pad clusters.
- Reference text (`U1`, `U9`, etc.) was always defined in the footprints (I checked — `reftext=1` on all of them) — if it's still not visible after the update, it's likely just hidden or set to a tiny size; right-click the part → **Properties** → check the Reference field's "Show" checkbox and font size.
- Save (Ctrl+S) once you've confirmed it looks right.

- **Red mask:** mask colour has essentially no effect on how a board handles heat. I kept red because you asked for it, and it's harmless.

import numpy as np, math, json, sys, os, time

RODS=[(47,0),(0,47),(-47,0),(0,-47)]
ax=np.arange(-H,H+STEP/2,STEP); X,Y=np.meshgrid(ax,ax,indexing='ij'); N=len(ax)
REFS=list(G); SIZE={r:(G[r]['w'],G[r]['h']) for r in REFS}
_base={}
def base(w,h):
    k=(round(w,3),round(h,3))
    if k in _base: return _base[k]

    dx=np.maximum(np.abs(X)-w/2,0); dy=np.maximum(np.abs(Y)-h/2,0); m&amp;=np.hypot(dx,dy)&gt;=ANT
    for rx,ry in RODS:
        dx=np.maximum(np.abs(X-rx)-w/2,0); dy=np.maximum(np.abs(Y-ry)-h/2,0); m&amp;=np.hypot(dx,dy)&gt;=ROD
    _base[k]=m; return m
def idx(v): return int(round((v+H)/STEP))
def rect(ref,p):
    w,h=SIZE[ref]
    if p[2]: w,h=h,w
    return p[0]-w/2,p[1]-h/2,p[0]+w/2,p[1]+h/2
def corridors(pl):
    if 'U1' not in pl: return []
    x0,y0,x1,y1=rect('U1',pl['U1']); horiz=(x1-x0)&gt;(y1-y0)
    if horiz: return [(x1,y0-1,H,y1+1),(-H,y0-1,x0,y1+1)]
    return [(x0-1,y1,x1+1,H),(x0-1,-H,x1+1,y0)]
def feasible(ref,rot,pl):
    w,h=SIZE[ref]
    if rot: w,h=h,w
    m=base(w,h).copy()
    blk=[rect(k,v) for k,v in pl.items() if k!=ref]
    if ref!='U1': blk+=corridors({k:v for k,v in pl.items() if k!=ref})
    for (x0,y0,x1,y1) in blk:
        i0=max(idx(x0-w/2-GAP)+1,0); i1=idx(x1+w/2+GAP)-1; j0=max(idx(y0-h/2-GAP)+1,0); j1=idx(y1+h/2+GAP)-1
        if i1&gt;=i0 and j1&gt;=j0: m[i0:i1+1,j0:j1+1]=False
    return m
def insert(ref,pl,rng):
    best=None
    for rot in rng.permutation([0,1]):
        m=feasible(ref,int(rot),pl)
        c=np.flatnonzero(m)
        if c.size==0: continue
        th=rng.uniform(0,2*math.pi); u=(math.cos(th),math.sin(th))
        s=(X.ravel()[c]*u[0]+Y.ravel()[c]*u[1])+rng.random(c.size)*3
        k=c[np.argmax(s)]; i,j=np.unravel_index(k,(N,N))
        cand=(float(X[i,j]),float(Y[i,j]),int(rot))
        if best is None or rng.random()&lt;0.5: best=cand
    return best
AREA={r:SIZE[r][0]*SIZE[r][1] for r in REFS}
def lns(seed,tlimit):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed); t0=time.time(); pl={}
    order=sorted(REFS,key=lambda r:-AREA[r])
    for r in order:
        c=insert(r,pl,rng)
        if c: pl[r]=c
    it=0
    while len(pl)&lt;len(REFS) and time.time()-t0&lt;tlimit:
        it+=1; old=dict(pl)
        k=int(rng.integers(1,4)); keys=list(pl)
        if keys:
            pick=[keys[int(rng.integers(len(keys)))]]
            if k&gt;1:   # also remove neighbours of the first (spatially local ruin)
                x,y=pl[pick[0]][:2]
                near=sorted(keys,key=lambda r:math.hypot(pl[r][0]-x,pl[r][1]-y))
                pick=near[:k]
            for r in pick: pl.pop(r,None)
        missing=[r for r in REFS if r not in pl]
        missing.sort(key=lambda r:-AREA[r]*(0.6+0.8*rng.random()))
        for r in missing:
            c=insert(r,pl,rng)
            if c: pl[r]=c
        if len(pl)&lt;len(old): pl=old
    return pl if len(pl)==len(REFS) else None, len(pl), it
# ---------- continuous hard-gated polish ----------
import random
def selfv(r,p):

    d=math.hypot(max(x0,0,-x1),max(y0,0,-y1))

    ox=min(a[2],b[2])+GAP-max(a[0],b[0]); oy=min(a[3],b[3])+GAP-max(a[1],b[1])

def itemv(pl,k):
    c=selfv(k,pl[k]); r=rect(k,pl[k])
    for o,q in pl.items():
        if o!=k: c+=ovl(r,rect(o,q))
    return c
NEAR={'R1':'U5','R2':'U2','R3':'U7','R4':'J5','R5':'J6','R6':'U8','R7':'U8','R8':'U8','R9':'U8','C1':'U2','C2':'U9','C3':'U7','U8':'J5'}
def soft(pl):
    c=0.0; T=pl['U1']; tx0,ty0,tx1,ty1=rect('U1',T); horiz=(tx1-tx0)&gt;(ty1-ty0)
    ends=[(tx0,T[1]),(tx1,T[1])] if horiz else [(T[0],ty0),(T[0],ty1)]
    for ex,ey in ends:
        c-=(R_IN-math.hypot(ex,ey))*7
        for k,q in pl.items():
            if k=='U1': continue
            x0,y0,x1,y1=rect(k,q)
            if horiz:
                if y1&gt;ty0-1 and y0&lt;ty1+1 and ((ex&gt;T[0] and x1&gt;ex) or (ex&lt;T[0] and x0&lt;ex)): c+=250
            else:
                if x1&gt;tx0-1 and x0&lt;tx1+1 and ((ey&gt;T[1] and y1&gt;ey) or (ey&lt;T[1] and y0&lt;ey)): c+=250
    rx,ry=pl['U2'][:2]; c+=abs(math.hypot(rx,ry)-21)*9
    gx,gy=pl['U3'][:2]; c-=min(math.hypot(gx-rx,gy-ry),50)*3
    ax_,ay_=pl['U9'][:2]; c-=min(math.hypot(ax_-gx,ay_-gy),48)*3
    bx,by=pl['U4'][:2]; c-=min(math.hypot(ax_-bx,ay_-by),36)*2
    for k in ('U2','U5','U7'): c+=max(0,math.hypot(pl[k][0]-T[0],pl[k][1]-T[1])-36)*5
    for k,n in NEAR.items(): c+=max(0,math.hypot(pl[k][0]-pl[n][0],pl[k][1]-pl[n][1])-16)*3
    for k,q in pl.items():
        if k[0]!='J': continue
        x0,y0,x1,y1=rect(k,q); c+=(R_IN-max(math.hypot(cx,cy) for cx in(x0,x1) for cy in(y0,y1)))*4
    return c
def polish(pl,seed,Nit=60000):
    rnd=random.Random(seed); pl=dict(pl); cur=soft(pl); best=dict(pl); bc=cur; T0,T1=40.0,0.05; keys=list(pl)
    for it in range(Nit):
        T=T0*(T1/T0)**(it/Nit); k=rnd.choice(keys); old=pl[k]
        new=(old[0],old[1],1-old[2]) if rnd.random()&lt;0.06 else (old[0]+rnd.gauss(0,0.3+3*T/T0),old[1]+rnd.gauss(0,0.3+3*T/T0),old[2])
        pl[k]=new
        if itemv(pl,k)&gt;1e-9: pl[k]=old; continue
        n=soft(pl)
        if n&lt;=cur or rnd.random()&lt;math.exp(-(n-cur)/max(T,1e-9)):
            cur=n
            if n&lt;bc: bc=n; best=dict(pl)
        else: pl[k]=old
    return best,bc
def job(a):
    seed,tl=a; pl,n,it=lns(seed,tl)
    if pl is None: return (seed,None,None,n,it)
    b,c=polish(pl,seed); return (seed,b,c,n,it)
def verify(pl):
    bad=[]
    for k in pl:
        if selfv(k,pl[k])&gt;1e-9: bad.append(k)
        for o in pl:
            if o&lt;k and ovl(rect(k,pl[k]),rect(o,pl[o]))&gt;1e-9: bad.append(k+'~'+o)
    return bad
if __name__=="__main__":
    tl=float(sys.argv[3]); t0=time.time(); area=sum(AREA.values())
    print(f"{len(REFS)} parts, {area:.0f} mm2 ({100*area/(math.pi*54**2):.0f}% of usable disc); LNS {tl:.0f}s x {os.cpu_count()-1} workers",flush=True)
    best=None
    with Pool(os.cpu_count()-1) as pool:
        for seed,b,c,n,it in pool.imap_unordered(job,[(s,tl) for s in range(1,(os.cpu_count()-1)*2+1)]):
            print(f"  worker seed {seed:2d}: placed {n}/{len(REFS)} after {it} ruin-recreate steps"+("" if b is None else f"  -&gt; COMPLETE soft={c:.0f}")+f"  ({time.time()-t0:.0f}s)",flush=True)
            if b is not None and (best is None or c&lt;best[1]): best=(b,c)
    if not best: sys.exit("no complete packing found")
    pl,c=best; bad=verify(pl); print("independent re-verification:", "ALL CLEAR" if not bad else bad)
    json.dump({k:[v[0],v[1],90 if v[2] else 0] for k,v in pl.items()},open(sys.argv[2],"w"),indent=1); print("saved, soft",round(c))

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-02T09:59:12)

Mostly no, with one real exception that I missed.

**Why most don't need one:** your sensor modules (BMP585, LSM6DSO32, L89, Core1121, W25Q64 and the MAX98357A amp) are finished breakout boards. Vendors normally put their own small bypass capacitors next to the chip, so a second 100 nF on your carrier board just duplicates it. I haven't seen your physical boards, so this is "normally true", not "verified". The fastest check is to look at each module for a tiny capacitor next to its VCC pin, or open the vendor's schematic. For some 7Semi boards I'm less sure, for example the flash. That is why `C3` is already there as cheap insurance.

**What your three capacitors do:**
- **C1** (10 µF): extra reserve for the radio's transmit bursts.
- **C2** (100 µF): extra reserve for the speaker amp.
- **C3** (100 nF): flash decoupling.

**The exception: the TPL5010 watchdog (U8) has no capacitor.** It is a bare SOT-23-6 chip you solder yourself, not a module. TI's datasheet calls for about 100 nF right at its supply pin. My own earlier list had this as `C13`, and it got lost when we trimmed the capacitor count. Without it, the watchdog can misbehave when the supply is noisy. That is the one part whose failure would break the failover you designed.

**Recommended fix:** add `C4`, 100 nF, from U8 pin 1 (+3V3) to pin 2 (GND), placed right beside U8. I'd use a small SMD 0805 ceramic (`Capacitor_SMD:C_0805_2012Metric`), because a short loop matters more here than staying through-hole. U8 is already surface-mount, so it is easy to hand-solder.

**Optional:** you can add 100 nF ceramic pads, left unfitted, near modules you're unsure about. They cost a little board area, since the board is already 64% full, so I'd only do this after you've checked the real boards.

If you want `C4` added, close KiCad and tell me. I'll add it to the schematic and board, place it next to U8, and re-run ERC, DRC and the parity check on the live files, as before.

**One thing I tried and backed out:** I attempted to pre-route the four decoupling connections for you. The straight-line tracks shorted against U8 pad 3, because SOT-23-6 pads are close together. I removed every track and via, so the board now has none at all. Route that loop by hand with the router's "walk around" mode on — it's four short tracks and it's described first in the guide.

**Routing guide: [08_ROUTING.md](VRITRA_Board1_rev7/08_ROUTING.md)** (replaces the old `04_ROUTING.md`, which had wrong reference designators). It covers:

- Track widths come from the net class automatically — 0.6 mm for power, 0.25 mm for signals. Don't type widths by hand.
- **Only 4 pads need power routing.** Every through-hole GND and +3V3 pad is already connected by the filled planes. The four exceptions are U8's and C4's surface-mount pads, which need a via each.
- Routing order: the decoupling loop, then +5V, then SPI as a group, chip selects, radio control, I²S, I²C, UART/watchdog, and the rest.
- Don't route on the `GND` or `3V3` layers — they're planes, and cutting through one breaks the return path for every track crossing it.
- Keep I²S at least 5 mm from the GNSS and barometer.

Backups are in the `Board 1` folder as `_backup_before_C4_*`. You can open KiCad and start routing — there are no lock files.

Still open before you order boards: the 94 mm rod circle and the spacer-versus-nut question, both of which affect the layout.

| U3 GNSS ↔ U7 flash (SPI noise) | **10.8 mm** |
| U2 radio ↔ U1 Teensy | 21.9 mm |
| U2 ↔ U3 | 46.8 mm ✓ |

The cause was in my own solver: the "keep passives near their chip" term had a 16 mm dead zone and a weight of 3, while the connector-to-rim term had weight 4. Rim placement outvoted decoupling. The capacitors were also placed last, into whatever gaps were left.

I'm re-solving now with the rules you asked for, this time properly:

- **Decoupling is a hard constraint, not a preference** — measured pad-to-pad, not centre-to-centre. C1→U2 ≤ 11 mm, C2→U9 ≤ 11 mm, C3→U7 ≤ 10 mm, C4→U8 ≤ 6 mm. Any layout that misses is thrown away.
- **Each capacitor is placed immediately after its chip**, so it claims adjacent space instead of scavenging leftovers.
- **U2 and U3 are placed second**, pushed to the rim and away from each other and the Teensy.
- **U1, U7, U8, U9 are then actively repelled** from both RF modules.
- All four rotations are allowed now, not just 0 and 90, which gives the capacitors more ways to sit close.

The search is running. I'll verify the result with KiCad's DRC before touching your project.

import numpy as np, math, json, sys, os, time
from multiprocessing import Pool
G=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
R_IN=54.0; GAP=0.6; ANT=8.0; ROD=7.5; STEP=0.5; H=55.0
RODS=[(47,0),(0,47),(-47,0),(0,-47)]
ax=np.arange(-H,H+STEP/2,STEP); X,Y=np.meshgrid(ax,ax,indexing='ij'); N=len(ax)
REFS=list(G); ROTS=(0,90,180,270)
def WH(r,rot): e=G[r][str(rot)]; return e['w'],e['h']
def padoff(r,rot,pad):
    p=G[r][str(rot)]['pads'].get(pad); return p
# decoupling pairs: cap pad -&gt; IC supply pad   (hard max distance)
PAIR=[("C1","1","U2","16",11.0),   # 10uF  -&gt; radio +3V3
      ("C2","1","U9","7", 11.0),   # 100uF -&gt; amp   +5V
      ("C3","1","U7","1", 10.0),   # 100nF -&gt; flash +3V3
      ("C4","1","U8","1",  6.0)]   # 100nF -&gt; watchdog +3V3 (SMD, keep very tight)
RF=("U2","U3"); NOISY=("U1","U7","U8","U9")
_base={}
def base(w,h):
    k=(round(w,3),round(h,3))
    if k in _base: return _base[k]
    m=((np.abs(X)+w/2)**2+(np.abs(Y)+h/2)**2)&lt;=R_IN**2
    dx=np.maximum(np.abs(X)-w/2,0); dy=np.maximum(np.abs(Y)-h/2,0); m&amp;=np.hypot(dx,dy)&gt;=ANT
    for rx,ry in RODS:
        dx=np.maximum(np.abs(X-rx)-w/2,0); dy=np.maximum(np.abs(Y-ry)-h/2,0); m&amp;=np.hypot(dx,dy)&gt;=ROD
    _base[k]=m; return m
def idx(v): return int(round((v+H)/STEP))
def rect(r,p):
    w,h=WH(r,p[2]); return p[0]-w/2,p[1]-h/2,p[0]+w/2,p[1]+h/2
def corridors(pl):
    if 'U1' not in pl: return []
    x0,y0,x1,y1=rect('U1',pl['U1']); horiz=(x1-x0)&gt;(y1-y0)
    if horiz: return [(x1,y0-1,H,y1+1),(-H,y0-1,x0,y1+1)]
    return [(x0-1,y1,x1+1,H),(x0-1,-H,x1+1,y0)]
def mask(r,rot,pl,skip=None):
    w,h=WH(r,rot); m=base(w,h).copy()
    blk=[rect(k,v) for k,v in pl.items() if k!=skip]
    if r!='U1': blk+=corridors(pl)
    for (x0,y0,x1,y1) in blk:
        i0=max(idx(x0-w/2-GAP)+1,0); i1=idx(x1+w/2+GAP)-1; j0=max(idx(y0-h/2-GAP)+1,0); j1=idx(y1+h/2+GAP)-1
        if i1&gt;=i0 and j1&gt;=j0: m[i0:i1+1,j0:j1+1]=False
    return m
def pad_xy(r,p,pad):
    o=padoff(r,p[2],pad); return (p[0]+o[0],p[1]+o[1])
def place(r,pl,rng,score,topk=8,rots=ROTS):
    best=None
    for rot in rots:
        m=mask(r,rot,pl)
        c=np.flatnonzero(m)
        if c.size==0: continue
        s=score(rot)
        v=s.ravel()[c]; k=min(topk,c.size)
        cand=c[np.argpartition(v,k-1)[:k]]
        pick=cand[rng.integers(len(cand))]
        i,j=np.unravel_index(pick,(N,N)); sc=s[i,j]
        if best is None or sc&lt;best[0]: best=(sc,(float(X[i,j]),float(Y[i,j]),rot))
    if best is None: return False
    pl[r]=best[1]; return True
def attempt(seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed); pl={}
    nz=lambda s: rng.random((N,N))*s
    Rr=np.hypot(X,Y)
    # 1 Teensy: anywhere legal, prefer not hugging the rim (leave rim for RF+connectors)
    if not place('U1',pl,rng,lambda rot: nz(2)+np.abs(Rr-14)*0.25,topk=120): return None
    # 2 RF modules: push to the rim, far from each other and from the Teensy
    def rim(rot,r):
        w,h=WH(r,rot); return -(np.hypot(np.abs(X)+w/2,np.abs(Y)+h/2))*1.2
    if not place('U3',pl,rng,lambda rot: rim(rot,'U3')+nz(2)
                 -np.hypot(X-pl['U1'][0],Y-pl['U1'][1])*0.5,topk=25): return None
    if not place('U2',pl,rng,lambda rot: rim(rot,'U2')+nz(2)
                 -np.hypot(X-pl['U3'][0],Y-pl['U3'][1])*0.9
                 -np.hypot(X-pl['U1'][0],Y-pl['U1'][1])*0.3,topk=25): return None
    # 3 remaining ICs: keep noisy ones away from both RF modules
    def awayRF(rot):
        s=np.zeros_like(X)
        for q in RF:
            if q in pl: s-=np.minimum(np.hypot(X-pl[q][0],Y-pl[q][1]),45)*0.8
        return s
    for r in ('U9','U7','U5','U4','U8'):
        if not place(r,pl,rng,lambda rot,r=r: awayRF(rot)+nz(4),topk=12): return None
    # 4 each decoupling cap: hard-pinned next to its IC's supply pad
    for cap,cp,ic,ip,lim in PAIR:
        tgt=pad_xy(ic,pl[ic],ip)
        def sc(rot,cap=cap,cp=cp,tgt=tgt):
            o=padoff(cap,rot,cp)
            return np.hypot((X+o[0])-tgt[0],(Y+o[1])-tgt[1])
        if not place(cap,pl,rng,sc,topk=2): return None
        if math.hypot(*(a-b for a,b in zip(pad_xy(cap,pl[cap],cp),tgt)))&gt;lim: return None
    # 5 connectors to the rim
    for r in ('J8','J5','J3','J7','J1','J2','J4','J6'):
        if not place(r,pl,rng,lambda rot,r=r: rim(rot,r)+nz(2),topk=8): return None
    # 6 resistors near what they serve
    AN={'R1':('U5','7'),'R2':('U2','3'),'R3':('U7','2'),'R4':('J5','1'),'R5':('J6','1'),
        'R6':('U8','3'),'R7':('U8','3'),'R8':('U8','4'),'R9':('U8','6')}
    for r in ('R6','R7','R8','R9','R4','R5','R1','R2','R3'):
        ic,ip=AN[r]; tgt=pad_xy(ic,pl[ic],ip)
        if not place(r,pl,rng,lambda rot,t=tgt: np.hypot(X-t[0],Y-t[1])+nz(1),topk=6): return None
    return pl
def soft(pl):
    c=0.0
    for cap,cp,ic,ip,lim in PAIR:
        c+=math.hypot(*(a-b for a,b in zip(pad_xy(cap,pl[cap],cp),pad_xy(ic,pl[ic],ip))))*12
    for q in RF:
        w,h=WH(q,pl[q][2]); c-=math.hypot(abs(pl[q][0])+w/2,abs(pl[q][1])+h/2)*6
        for n in NOISY: c-=min(math.hypot(pl[q][0]-pl[n][0],pl[q][1]-pl[n][1]),45)*2.5
    c-=min(math.hypot(pl['U2'][0]-pl['U3'][0],pl['U2'][1]-pl['U3'][1]),60)*3
    for k in ('U2','U5','U7'):
        c+=max(0,math.hypot(pl[k][0]-pl['U1'][0],pl[k][1]-pl['U1'][1])-40)*4
    for k in pl:
        if k[0]!='J': continue
        w,h=WH(k,pl[k][2]); c+=(R_IN-math.hypot(abs(pl[k][0])+w/2,abs(pl[k][1])+h/2))*4
    return c
def selfv(r,p):
    x0,y0,x1,y1=rect(r,p); c=0.0
    for cx in(x0,x1):
        for cy in(y0,y1):
            d=math.hypot(cx,cy)
            if d&gt;R_IN: c+=(d-R_IN)**2
    d=math.hypot(max(x0,0,-x1),max(y0,0,-y1))
    if d&lt;ANT: c+=(ANT-d)**2
    for rx,ry in RODS:
        d=math.hypot(max(x0-rx,0,rx-x1),max(y0-ry,0,ry-y1))
        if d&lt;ROD: c+=(ROD-d)**2
    x0,y0,x1,y1=rect('U1',p) if r=='U1' else (0,0,0,0)
    return c
def ovl(a,b):
    ox=min(a[2],b[2])+GAP-max(a[0],b[0]); oy=min(a[3],b[3])+GAP-max(a[1],b[1])
    return ox*oy if ox&gt;0 and oy&gt;0 else 0.0
def itemv(pl,k):
    c=selfv(k,pl[k]); r=rect(k,pl[k])
    for o,q in pl.items():
        if o!=k: c+=ovl(r,rect(o,q))
    if k!='U1':
        for cr in corridors(pl): c+=ovl(r,cr)
    return c
def hard_ok(pl):
    bad=[]
    for k in pl:
        if itemv(pl,k)&gt;1e-9: bad.append(k)
    for cap,cp,ic,ip,lim in PAIR:
        dd=math.hypot(*(a-b for a,b in zip(pad_xy(cap,pl[cap],cp),pad_xy(ic,pl[ic],ip))))
        if dd&gt;lim+1e-6: bad.append(f"{cap}-{ic} {dd:.1f}&gt;{lim}")
    return bad
import random
def polish(pl,seed,Nit=45000):
    rnd=random.Random(seed); pl=dict(pl); cur=soft(pl); best=dict(pl); bc=cur; T0,T1=30.0,0.05; keys=list(pl)
    for it in range(Nit):
        T=T0*(T1/T0)**(it/Nit); k=rnd.choice(keys); old=pl[k]
        if rnd.random()&lt;0.08: new=(old[0],old[1],ROTS[rnd.randrange(4)])
        else: new=(old[0]+rnd.gauss(0,0.3+2.5*T/T0),old[1]+rnd.gauss(0,0.3+2.5*T/T0),old[2])
        pl[k]=new
        if itemv(pl,k)&gt;1e-9 or hard_ok(pl): pl[k]=old; continue
        n=soft(pl)
        if n&lt;=cur or rnd.random()&lt;math.exp(-(n-cur)/max(T,1e-9)):
            cur=n
            if n&lt;bc: bc=n; best=dict(pl)
        else: pl[k]=old
    return best,bc
def job(seed):
    for t in range(60):
        pl=attempt(seed*1000+t)
        if pl and not hard_ok(pl):
            b,c=polish(pl,seed); return (seed,b,c)
    return (seed,None,None)
if __name__=="__main__":
    t0=time.time(); best=None; ok=0
    with Pool(os.cpu_count()-1) as pool:
        for seed,b,c in pool.imap_unordered(job,range(1,(os.cpu_count()-1)*3+1)):
            if b is None: continue

    if not best: sys.exit("no layout met the decoupling + geometry constraints")

    print("RF:")

        for n in NOISY: print(f"    {q}&lt;-&gt;{n}: {math.hypot(pl[q][0]-pl[n][0],pl[q][1]-pl[n][1]):.1f} mm")

    json.dump({k:[v[0],v[1],v[2]] for k,v in pl.items()},open(sys.argv[2],"w"),indent=1)
    print("saved",sys.argv[2])

The two 100 nF bypass caps are the ones where distance really matters (loop inductance), and both are now very tight. C1 and C2 are bulk reservoirs, where a few mm costs little.

**Done:**
- I read the official KiCad 10 routing documentation (docs.kicad.org/10.0/en/pcbnew/pcbnew.html) and confirmed every hotkey below against it.
- I extracted all 77 nets and their pad coordinates from your live board. About 50 of them are `unconnected-*` nets, which are the pins you deliberately left unused. Ignore those.

**Not done:** the written step-by-step guide file, and a route-map picture with airwires coloured by net group. Ask me for those next. The plan below is the working version.

## Setup (once)
1. **Route → Interactive Router Settings:** set Mode to **Walk Around** (or **Shove**; the docs recommend one of these). The router then refuses to create DRC violations.
2. **Top toolbar:** set track width to **use netclass width** and via size to **use netclass sizes**. That gives 0.6 mm for power and 0.25 mm for signals.
3. Turn on the ratsnest with its left-toolbar icon.

## Routing keys (from the docs)
| Key | Action |
|---|---|
| **X** | Start routing, then click a pad |
| **V** | Place a via and switch layer |
| **PgUp / PgDn** | Select F.Cu / B.Cu directly |
| **/** | Flip the 45° track posture |
| **Backspace** | Undo the last segment |
| **End** | Finish the route |
| **Esc** | Exit the router |
| **Ctrl / Shift** (held) | Disable grid / object snapping |
| **\`** (backtick) | Highlight the selected net |
| **B** | Fill all zones |

- **F.Cu** is the main signal layer.
- **B.Cu** is the overflow layer.
- **GND** and **3V3** are planes: never route on them.

## Order, with your real pads
1. **C4 loop first.**
   - Route U8.1 (+3V3) to C4.1, and U8.2 (GND) to C4.2.
   - Put one via on each rail beside U8.
   - Go slowly: SOT-23-6 pads are close together, and my automatic attempt shorted against U8.3.
2. **+5V at 0.6 mm:** J1.1 → U1.VIN, U9.7, and C2.1.
3. **Other power pins:** every through-hole GND and +3V3 pad is already tied by the planes. Only U8 and C4 need vias.
4. **SPI, side by side on F.Cu, daisy-chained:**
   - SCK: U1.13 → U2.4, U5.4, U7.4
   - MOSI: U1.11 → U2.5, U5.5, U7.5
   - MISO: U1.12 → U2.6, U5.6, U7.3
5. **Chip selects with their pull-ups:**
   - LORA_CS: U1.10 → U2.3 and R2.1
   - LSM_CS: U1.2 → U5.7 and R1.1
   - FLASH_CS: U1.3 → U7.2 and R3.1
6. **Radio control:** U1.14 → U2.8, U1.15 → U2.11, U1.16 → U2.7.
7. **I²S:**
   - U1.20 → U9.1, U1.21 → U9.2, U1.7 → U9.3.
   - Keep these at least 5 mm from U3 (GNSS) and U4 (barometer).
8. **I²C:** SDA U1.18 → U4.6, J2.1, J7.1. SCL U1.19 → U4.4, J2.2, J7.2.
9. **UART and watchdog:**
   - GPS_BCAST: U3.9 → U1.0, R4.1, R5.1
   - R4.2 → J5.1 and R5.2 → J6.1
   - GPS_TX: U1.1 → U3.10
   - PARA_TLM: J6.2, U1.28, J5.4
   - WDT_PET: U1.4, U8.4, R8.1, J5.3
   - WDT_RSTn: U8.6, R9.1, J5.2
   - Timing net: U8.3, R6.1, R7.1
10. **ENV and expansion header:**
    - ENV nets: U1.22 → J4.2, U1.17 → J4.1, U1.24 → J3.2, U1.25 → J3.1, U1.26 → J3.3.
    - GPIO6/8/9/27/29/30/31/32 → J8 pins 2–9.
11. **Finish:** press **B**, then run DRC with the parity box ticked. Target is 0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 unconnected.

## Still open before ordering boards

- Buy C2 as a Ø6.3 mm 100 µF part.
