from pathlib import Path
```

`import math,json`
`import pcbnew as p`
`R=Path(__file__).parent`
`b=p.LoadBoard(str(R/'Board1.kicad_pcb'))`
`mm=p.ToMM`
`def xy(v): return mm(v.x),mm(v.y)`
`def pt(x,y): return p.VECTOR2I(p.FromMM(x),p.FromMM(y))`
`zones=list(b.Zones()); source=next(z for z in zones if z.GetNetname()=='GND')`
`for layer in [p.F_Cu,p.B_Cu]:`
` z=p.Cast_to_ZONE(source.Duplicate(False)); z.SetLayer(layer); z.SetIslandRemovalMode(p.ISLAND_REMOVAL_MODE_ALWAYS); z.SetLocalClearance(p.FromMM(.33)); z.SetThermalReliefSpokeWidth(p.FromMM(.25)); b.Add(z)`
`tracks=list(b.GetTracks()); pads=[pad for f in b.GetFootprints() for pad in f.Pads()]`
`obstacles=[]`
`for z in zones:`
` if z.GetIsRuleArea():`
`  bb=z.GetBoundingBox(); obstacles.append(tuple(mm(v) for v in (bb.GetX(),bb.GetY(),bb.GetRight(),bb.GetBottom())))`
`def segdist(x,y,a,c):`
` dx,dy=c[0]-a[0],c[1]-a[1]; dd=dx*dx+dy*dy`
` t=max(0,min(1,((x-a[0])*dx+(y-a[1])*dy)/dd)) if dd else 0`
` return math.hypot(x-a[0]-t*dx,y-a[1]-t*dy)`
`added=[]`
`def clear(x,y):`
` if math.hypot(x-100,y-100)>53.6:return False`
` for x1,y1,x2,y2 in obstacles:`
`  if x1-.5<x<x2+.5 and y1-.5<y<y2+.5:return False`
` for pad in pads:`
`  bb=pad.GetBoundingBox(); margin=.78 if pad.GetNetname()!='GND' else .7`
`  if mm(bb.GetX())-margin<x<mm(bb.GetRight())+margin and mm(bb.GetY())-margin<y<mm(bb.GetBottom())+margin:return False`
` for t in tracks:`
`  if type(t)==p.PCB_VIA:`
`   if math.dist((x,y),xy(t.GetPosition()))<1.1:return False`
`  elif t.GetNetname()!='GND' and segdist(x,y,xy(t.GetStart()),xy(t.GetEnd()))<.4+.34+mm(t.GetWidth())/2:return False`
` if any(math.dist((x,y),(a['x'],a['y']))<1.3 for a in added):return False`
` return True`
`def add(x,y,label):`
` if not clear(x,y):return False`
` v=p.PCB_VIA(b); v.SetPosition(pt(x,y)); v.SetWidth(p.FromMM(.8)); v.SetDrill(p.FromMM(.4)); v.SetViaType(p.VIATYPE_THROUGH); v.SetLayerPair(p.F_Cu,p.B_Cu); v.SetNetCode(b.FindNet('GND').GetNetCode()); b.Add(v)`
` added.append(dict(x=x,y=y,label=label,uuid=v.m_Uuid.AsString())); return True`
`targets=[]`
`for f in b.GetFootprints():`
` if f.GetReference().startswith('U'):`
`  for pad in f.Pads():`
`   if pad.GetNetname()=='GND': targets.append((f.GetReference(),*xy(pad.GetPosition())))`
`for t in tracks:`
` if type(t)==p.PCB_VIA and t.GetNetname() not in ['GND','+3V3','+5V']: targets.append((t.GetNetname(),*xy(t.GetPosition())))`
`for label,x,y in targets:`
` done=False`
` for radius in [1.6,2,2.5,3]:`
`  for i in range(16):`
`   a=i*math.pi/8`
`   if add(round(x+radius*math.cos(a),4),round(y+radius*math.sin(a),4),label):done=True;break`
`  if done:break`
` if not done: print('No nearby stitching location:',label,x,y)`
`for angle in range(0,360,12):`
` a=math.radians(angle);add(round(100+51*math.cos(a),4),round(100+51*math.sin(a),4),'perimeter')`
`p.SaveBoard(str(R/'Board1.kicad_pcb'),b)`
`(R/'stitching.json').write_text(json.dumps(added,indent=2))`
`print('Added',len(added),'ground stitching vias')`

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-02T15:09:50)

The board is **fully routed and verified clean**. I need you to close KiCad before I can merge it into your live project.

## What I did

The previous session worked entirely on a copy in `Downloads/CHAT/pcb-routing` and **never merged anything back** — your live project still has only the 21 tracks you routed by hand. It stopped right after freerouting produced `repair.ses`, without importing it.

Picking up from there:

1. **Imported `repair.ses`** — that fixed GPIO29, taking unconnected from 9 down to 6.
2. **Wrote a grid maze router** for the rest. Freerouting couldn't finish them; the gaps were 10–35 mm, and GPS_BCAST had no tracks at all. My router bakes clearance into the obstacle grid, so any path it returns is legal by construction.
3. **Found and fixed a bug in my own router**: I used unrotated pad sizes, which let a FLASH_CS track get too close to a rotated J5 pad. Switched to rotation-aware bounding boxes and rerouted.
4. **Removed leftover dangling stubs.**

## Final state, verified by KiCad's own tools

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| DRC violations | **0** |
| Unconnected | **0** |
| Schematic ↔ PCB parity | **0** |
| KiCad connectivity engine | **0 unconnected** |
| Gerbers + drill export | 14 files, OK |

535 tracks, 69 vias, 3342 mm of copper. Signals on F.Cu (315) and B.Cu (220) only — **nothing on the GND or 3V3 planes**. Track widths are 0.25 mm signal and 0.6 mm power, from the net classes. Closest copper to the board edge is 0.64 mm. Stackup, red mask, ENIG, all five holes and the Ø110 outline are intact.

It passes DRC with **both** your live 0.2 mm rules and the tighter 0.33 mm Robu rules.

## One judgement call

I **left out the outer GND pours** on F.Cu/B.Cu. They produced 7 `starved_thermal` warnings — pads reached by only one thermal spoke. You already have a solid inner GND plane 0.2 mm below F.Cu doing the return-path and shielding work, so the outer pours added little for real warnings. I tried making them pad-isolated shield fills with stitching vias instead, but pcbnew segfaulted, and I judged this not worth more time against your "zero errors" requirement. Say the word if you want me to retry.

## To merge — please do this

## What the previous session had actually left

It worked entirely on a **copy** in `Downloads/CHAT/pcb-routing` and never merged anything back — your live board still had only the 21 tracks you'd routed by hand. It stopped right after freerouting produced `repair.ses`, without importing it.

I imported that (fixed GPIO29), then routed the five nets freerouting couldn't finish — GPS_BCAST had **zero** tracks and the gaps were 10–35 mm, not small glitches — using a grid maze router that bakes clearance into the obstacle map, so paths are legal by construction.

## Verification on your live files

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Schematic ERC | **0** |
| PCB DRC (all severities) | **0** |
| Schematic ↔ PCB parity | **0** |
| KiCad connectivity engine | **0 unconnected** |
| All 38 multi-pad nets reachable pad-to-pad | **0 problems** |

535 tracks, 69 vias, 3342 mm of copper. F.Cu 315 / B.Cu 220 segments, and **nothing on the GND or 3V3 planes**.

## Specifically for soldering those modules

- **Annular rings:** smallest is 0.35 mm across all 170 plated holes, comfortably above the 0.15 mm IPC class-2 minimum. Hand-soldering headers will be fine.
- **Thermal relief:** both planes connect pads via thermal spokes (0.3 mm gap, 0.5 mm spoke), so your iron won't be fighting a copper heatsink on GND and 3V3 pins.
- **Vias are tented on both sides**, so the vias under module bodies (19 under the Teensy) are covered by solder mask and can't short against a module.
- **Teensy supply:** VIN → +5V, all three GNDs connected, both 3V3 pins correctly left No-Connect. The +5V net's narrowest track is 0.6 mm, good for about 1.7 A — ample for the Teensy's ~100 mA plus the amp's ~1 A peak.
- **Radio antenna:** U2 pads 1 and 9 are unrouted, matching the u.FL sockets visible on your Core1121-HF photo.

Fabrication package is in `Board 1/fab_rev7_routed` (20 files: gerbers, PTH + NPTH drill with maps, placement CSV, BOM, and the DRC/ERC reports). Non-plated drills come out as 4 × 8.5 mm and 1 × 13 mm, as intended. Clearance is set to 0.33 mm for Robu. Backup of your pre-routing files is in `_backup_before_routing_20261002_204259`.

## Two things I did not do, deliberately

1. **No outer GND pours on F.Cu/B.Cu.** They produced 7 `starved_thermal` warnings, and the shield-fill-plus-stitching alternative segfaulted pcbnew. The solid inner GND plane sits 0.2 mm below F.Cu and already provides the return path and shielding, so I judged the pours not worth warnings on a board you need clean.
2. **I could not verify the module footprints against physical parts.** This is the one real risk left before you spend money. Print the board 1:1 and check these against the actual modules, because a wrong row spacing means the module won't fit:
   - **U2 Core1121** — 2×8, 2.54 mm pitch, **15.24 mm** between rows (consistent with your photo)
   - **U3 L89** — 2×6, **12.7 mm** between rows
   - **U5 LSM6DSO32** — 14 pads, **12.7 mm** between rows

Also still open: the 94 mm rod bolt circle and whether the rods use spacers or M8 nuts.

One assembly reminder: **cut the VIN–VUSB pad bridge on the Teensy** before powering it from the +5V rail, or USB and the EPS will fight.

I checked your work first, as you asked. Board 1 is ready to send to Robu once you've done the 1:1 fit check. Two problems turned up: one I caused and undid, and one real fabrication problem that I fixed.

**What happened to your files.** At 01:32 a script of mine overwrote the PCB and created 11 errors. I restored your own 01:20 KiCad save from KiCad's `.history` folder, so none of your work was lost. That save has all the rev9 changes plus your "Please Work" silkscreen text.

**What I fixed**
- **The five big holes would have been a problem at Robu.** The 8.5 mm rod holes and the 13 mm antenna hole were drilled holes, but Robu's maximum drill size is 6.30 mm. They are now milled cut-outs on the outline layer, which is what Robu asks for, and I changed the library footprints too.
- **The old rev9 package no longer matched the board**, because it was exported before your silkscreen edit. I made a new one.

**Results on the final board**

| Check | Result | Robu limit |
|---|---|---|
| ERC / DRC with all 62 rules as errors / unconnected / schematic match | all **0** | — |
| Every pin (190) compared to its schematic net | identical | — |
| Every pad of all 45 multi-pad nets connected | yes | — |
| Smallest track / smallest gap | 0.25 / 0.33 mm | 0.09 / 0.09 |
| Via ring / plated pad ring | 0.15 / 0.35 mm | 0.13 / 0.25 |
| Closest holes on different nets | 0.69 mm | 0.50 |
| Hole sizes in the drill file | 0.30–1.00 mm (253 holes) | 0.20–6.30 |
| Gerbers re-read after export | 4 copper layers, Ø110 outline + 5 cut-outs | — |

The 5 V and 3.3 V pins of every module go to the right supply, and every GND pin that's wired goes to GND. U5's second GND pin and U4's 3V3 pin are left open on purpose. The small leftover pieces of the inner planes near U3 are attached to their nets, not floating copper.

**Decision for you: solder mask colour.** Choose green on the order form. Mask colour doesn't help with heat. U8 (the watchdog chip) has 0.250 mm of mask between its pins. Robu keeps that for green (minimum 0.20 mm) but needs 0.254 mm for red. With red it will still work, but check U8 for solder bridges after soldering.

**Cleanup.** The 11 backup folders, `fab_rev7/8/9`, `REV9_AUDIT.md` and `VRITRA_symbols.bak` went to the **Recycle Bin**, not permanent delete, so you can restore any of them. **CLAUDE** (2026-10-02T21:50:23)

I wrote the Board 2 guide in the same format as your rev6 guide. It covers the 7 environmental sensors from your handoff, but the **DS3231 does not fit on Board 2**, so it isn't on this board. One decision about the stack also has to be made before ordering.

**What's in it:** symbols, footprints, the schematic wire by wire, Board Setup copied from Board 1, outline and holes, placement coordinates, routing, inner planes, a full silkscreen list, strict DRC, Gerber export, the Robu order form, assembly order and bring-up.

**Changes from rev6 for Board 2, all based on the finished Board 1 or vendor drawings:**
- **Connectors are JST-EH**, matching the real Board 1. Board 2 J1 mates with Board 1 **J3 "ENV_D"** and J2 with **J4 "ENV_A"**; J3 takes +5V/+3V3/GND from Board 4.
- **SEN0290:** your dimension PDF shows IRQ is a **separate 2-pin GND/IRQ header**, not part of the Gravity socket. Rev6's 5-pin symbol was wrong. I read the hole and pad positions straight from the PDF's drawing data.
- **SCD41 has no solderable pins at all.** It's held by 4 × M3 screws and a latching PH2.0 cable, not a Dupont header that could fall off under shock.
- **BMV080:** DFRobot says the header goes on the module's back so the lens faces up, and CSB/MISO stay unconnected. Its I²C address is set by jumpers on the module itself.
- **Rod and antenna holes are milled cut-outs**, as fixed on Board 1 for Robu's 6.30 mm drill limit. **Every footprint has a courtyard**, the cause of Board 1's DRC problems.
- **Board 1 has no pull-ups on the Board 2 sensor bus** (ENV_SDA/ENV_SCL), so Board 2 has two optional 4.7 kΩ positions. You fit them only after measuring.
- **MQ-131:** both outputs reach 5 V. The digital output stays unconnected and the analog output goes through a 12k/18k divider (3.18 V worst case).

**Placement is checked, not guessed.** I solved it with the real part sizes:
- ≥ 1.0 mm between all parts and ≥ 2.1 mm from the edge.
- The MQ-131 heater is about 57 mm from the HDC3020 and 63 mm from the SCD41.
- The light sensors sit on one side; the Board 1 connectors are grouped on one edge.

**Decisions for you:**
1. **DS3231:** with it added, the solver found almost no legal layout: 0 with everything on the top side, and only 1 of 24 runs when I tried putting the SCD41 underneath (that one broke when snapped to the grid). I recommend mounting it on **Board 3**, cabled to Board 1's J7 "RTC" connector. It stays powered and read by the primary Teensy only.
2. **Board 2 must be the top plate.** The BMV080 needs **≥ 350 mm of clear space** in front of its lens, and the AS7343 and ML8511 need daylight. If the mechanical team can't put Board 2 at the top under an open, vented cap, those three sensors need to move to a pod on the shell.

**Still to measure:** the HDC3020, AS7343, ML8511 and MQ-131 header positions; 7Semi and Robu don't publish them. Section B.8.1 shows how. Space is reserved for each, and if a measured part comes out bigger than its space I'll re-run the placement.

I can also create the `Board 2` KiCad project for you with Board 1's setup, outline, holes and keep-outs already in place, so you start at the schematic.

Files are in `D:\linux\Software\Claude\VRITRA_Board2\`:
- VRITRA_Board2_KiCad10_Guide.md
- board2_placement.png
- board2_placement.svg

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T10:35:36)

Here is how to build the SEN0290 symbol. It's a 2-pin symbol, because only the 2-pin header goes through your board.

**Why only 2 pins.** DFRobot's schematic shows the module has two connectors:
- **P2** is the Gravity cable socket carrying SDA, SCL, GND and VCC. It sits on the module, so it never touches your PCB.
- **P3** is the 2-pin header carrying IRQ and GND. This one goes through your PCB.

Giving the symbol SDA/SCL/VCC pins would leave pins with no footprint pads. The cable gets its own connector (J6) in the schematic instead.

**Pin facts, from the schematic and the datasheet:**

| Connector | Pin | Signal | Electrical type |
|---|---|---|---|
| P3 (header) | 1, square pad | **IRQ** | Output |
| P3 (header) | 2, round pad | **GND** | Power input |

**Building the symbol in KiCad 10:**
1. Open the **Symbol Editor** and select `VRITRA_symbols` in the left list.
2. **File → New Symbol…**
   - Name: `SEN0290_IRQ2`
   - Reference designator: `U`
   - Units: 1
   - Derive from: leave empty
   - Click **OK**.
3. Set **View → Grid** to **50 mil (1.27 mm)**.
4. Press **P** (pin tool in this editor) and click inside the canvas. In the Pin Properties dialog enter:
   - Pin name: `IRQ`
   - Pin number: `1`
   - Electrical type: `Output`
   - Length: `100 mil`

   Click **OK**, then click to place it so the connection end points outward.
5. Press **P** again and place the second pin, pointing the same way:
   - Name `GND`
   - Number `2`
   - Type `Power input`
6. Draw the body with the rectangle tool, about 5 grid squares wide and 3 tall, around the two pins. Add a text label `SEN0290`.
7. Click **Symbol Properties** and set:
   - Value: `SEN0290`
   - Description: `DFRobot Gravity lightning sensor, GND/IRQ header only`
   - Datasheet: the product page link
8. Press **Ctrl+S**.

The footprint's pad 1 (square) must match the symbol's pin 1 (IRQ), and pad 2 (round) must match pin 2 (GND). My guide's B.6 gives the footprint coordinates.

**Pin order correction:** my first Board 2 guide had the Gravity cable pin order backwards. DFRobot's schematic numbers the cable pins **1 = SDA, 2 = SCL, 3 = GND, 4 = VCC**. The first guide wrote 1 = +3V3, 2 = GND, 3 = SCL, 4 = SDA, which could have put 3.3 V on the data line. I've fixed J5/J6 and the other affected tables. Cables are normally wired straight through, but check yours with a meter before powering up (E.2 step 3).

**Other things the schematic shows:**
- Both DIP switches **OFF** gives address **0x03**, the default. They are pulled up on the module.
- The module sets itself to I²C mode, so you don't need to do anything.
- The AS3935 chip has an internal 10 kΩ pull-up on SDA, and R3 looks like a 10 kΩ pull-up on SCL. That means the module already has pull-ups; I read R3 from a small drawing, so confirm it with a meter, as in A.4.

The guide is updated in `VRITRA_Board2_KiCad10_Guide.md`. I can also generate the symbol and footprint files for you to import, if you'd rather not draw them by hand.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T07:28:25)

For the HDC3020 you need two small things in the schematic: a pin-less symbol for the module, and a 4-pin connector for its cable.

## 1. Module symbol `Module_Mount` (shared with the SCD41)

Make it once; the SCD41 uses it too.

1. In the Board 2 Project Manager open the **Symbol Editor**. In the left tree select **VRITRA_symbols**. If it isn't listed, follow guide B.2 to add `${KIPRJMOD}/../Board 1/VRITRA_symbols.kicad_sym`.
2. Choose **File → New Symbol…** and enter:
   - Name: `Module_Mount`
   - Reference designator: `M`
   - Units: 1
   - Leave "Derive from existing symbol" empty.
   - Click **OK**.
3. Set the grid to **50 mil**.
4. Pick the **rectangle** tool in the right toolbar. Click one corner, then the opposite corner, making a box about 8 × 4 grid squares.
5. Pick the **text** tool, click inside the box and type `MOUNT`.
6. Open **Symbol Properties** and set:
   - Value: `Module_Mount`
   - Description: `Pin-less cable module, mounting holes only`
7. Do **not** add any pins. Press **Ctrl+S**.

There are no pins because the HDC3020 has no header. It connects only through its cable, so the board needs just two holes and an outline. That footprint is in guide B.8.2.

## 2. Cable connector J7 (nothing to draw)

Use KiCad's built-in `Connector_Generic:Conn_01x04`. In the Schematic Editor press **A**, type `Conn_01x04`, place it, and press **E** to set:
- Reference: `J7`
- Value: `HDC3020_CABLE`

Wire it in this order. It is **not** the same as J5 and J6:

| J7 pin | Connect to |
|---|---|
| 1 | label `ENV_SCL` |
| 2 | label `ENV_SDA` |
| 3 | `GND` |
| 4 | `+3V3` |

Place the module itself as `Module_Mount` (reference M2, value `HDC3020 7Semi`) and leave it with no wires.

## 3. Footprints

In **Tools → Assign Footprints…**:
- J7 gets `Connector_JST:JST_PH_B4B-PH-K_1x04_P2.00mm_Vertical`.
- M2 gets `HDC3020_7Semi_Mount`, which you still have to draw from guide B.8.2: two NPTH holes at (−7.25, ±6.00), plus the outline and courtyard.

## Optional: named pins on J7

Because the SCL/SDA order is easy to get backwards, you can build a custom 4-pin connector symbol instead of the generic one. Use the same Symbol Editor steps as above, with pins named SCL (1), SDA (2), GND (3) and 3V3 (4), all type **Passive**. The schematic then shows the order on its face.

Before you rely on this, check J7's pin order with a meter, as described in the guide. I took it from the photo's silkscreen because 7Semi publishes no schematic.

I can also generate the symbol file and the `HDC3020_7Semi_Mount` footprint file for you to import, if you'd rather not draw them by hand.

---
**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T08:07:49)

I'd **build the PCB for the flight board** and use a zero board only as a bench prototype. Board 2 is a small circuit, but it has to survive the flight.

**Why a zero board is risky for flight**
- **Vibration.** The rules require surviving 15 g launch and 30 g shock. Hand-soldered wire joints and Dupont jumper cables are the usual failure point. A loose SDA wire loses a sensor, and a loose +5V or GND wire could brown out the board.
- **It must be the structural plate.** Board 2 is a Ø110 mm disc on four rods, with holes at the same positions as the other boards. A rectangular zero board can't do that. You'd need a separate round plate, and then you're no longer saving anything.
- **Noise and grounding.** The PCB has a solid ground plane under the ML8511 and MQ-131 signals. On a zero board those analog lines run alongside the I²C wires and the 150 mA heater return.
- **Wiring mistakes.** There are already two cable-order traps. The HDC3020's SCL/SDA order is the opposite of the DFRobot modules, and the MQ-131's 5 V output must never reach the Teensy. A PCB fixes these once. Hand wiring lets any of them happen again.

**Where a zero board is useful**
- **Prove the pinouts before ordering.** Wire the modules on a zero board with short jumpers. Scan Wire2 and confirm the five addresses: 0x03, 0x39, 0x44, 0x57 and 0x62. That tests the cable orders, the pull-ups (measure them), and the MQ-131 divider.
- **Measure the unknown modules.** I still need real measurements of the AS7343, ML8511 and MQ-131 boards to finish the PCB footprints. Having them in your hands on a bench is the time to take those measurements.
- **Run the firmware.** Your Board 1 firmware can be tested against the real sensors before the PCB arrives.

**What I'd do**
1. Build the PCB as in the guide. The placement is already verified.
2. Build the zero-board prototype in parallel and use it to confirm the cables and measure the modules.
3. If the PCB isn't back in time for a test flight, fly the prototype with these safeguards:
   - **Solder every wire** and use no Dupont jumpers.
   - Fit **JST connectors** where the harness leaves the board.
   - Add **hot-glue strain relief** on every wire.
   - Mount it on a **round plate** with the same four rod holes.

One thing that would change my answer is the schedule. If the competition deadline makes waiting for a PCB impossible, tell me the dates and I'll plan the prototype as the flight board and list what to harden.

|---|---|
| Teensy 4.1 (backup) | Powered and running, but silent until it detects Board 1 has failed |
| ICM-20948 | Backup motion sensor for mandatory field 13 |
| BMP585 #2 | Backup altitude, pressure and temperature (fields 4–6) |
| LR1121 (Core1121) + RadioMaster antenna | Its own radio, so a dead Board 1 radio can't stop backup telemetry |
| Teensy's built-in microSD | Backup data logging |
| GNSS receive buffer | Takes the GPS data from Board 1 (see below) |

**What connects to it**
- **Board 1 J5 "BACKUP", 5 pins:**
  1. GPS data (through a 1 kΩ resistor)
  2. `WDT_RSTn` (watchdog fault)
  3. `WDT_PET` (heartbeat)
  4. `PARA_TLM` (spin rate from the parachute board)
  5. GND
- **Power from Board 4** (+5 V, +3V3_B, GND), with its own INA226 on a connector.
- **The DS3231 clock module.** I recommended moving it here from Board 2, but you haven't confirmed. It would be cabled to Board 1's J7, so the primary Teensy still reads it.

**It takes over like this**
- The watchdog on Board 1 raises a fault line, and Board 3 watches both that line and the heartbeat.
- On a confirmed failure, Board 3 starts transmitting on its own radio, using the GPS data it already receives.
- The old guide used a UART mux to switch GPS to Board 3. Your real Board 1 replaced that mux with a shared GPS line, so that part of the guide is out of date.

**Open points**
- The guide has no fixed stack order. Board 2 should be on top for its optical sensors. Board 3's antenna should not be blocked by another board. Tell me the mechanical order if you have one.
- Board 3's pin map in the guide predates the shared-GPS change. It needs updating before the schematic.

Want me to rewrite the Board 3 plan from your real Board 1 connectors?

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T09:19:00)

Yes, that image is the Teensy 4.0 pinout, and that board is the parachute board's controller (Board 5). Board 3 stays on the Teensy 4.1. A 4.0 has only 28 through-holes (two rows of 14), and your 4.1 symbol and footprint already use the same pin labels. The fastest and safest way is to **copy the 4.1 versions and trim off the extra pins**, not draw 28 new pins by hand.

**What the 4.0 needs**

| | Left column / bottom row | Right column / top row |
|---|---|---|
| Pins | `GND2, 0, 1 … 12` (14) | `VIN, GND1, 3V3_1, 23, 22 … 13` (14) |
| Delete from the 4.1 copy | `3V3_2, 24 … 32` | `GND3, 41 … 33` |

Pins 24–33 on a real Teensy 4.0 are underside pads with no holes, so they have nothing to solder to.

## Part 1: Symbol (Symbol Editor)

1. Open the **Symbol Editor** from the Project Manager. In the left list, open **VRITRA_symbols** and double-click **Teensy_4.1**.
2. Choose **File → Save As…**. Select library **VRITRA_symbols**, name it `Teensy_4.0`, and click **OK**. Check the top bar now says `Teensy_4.0`, not `Teensy_4.1`; if it still says 4.1, stop before deleting anything.
3. Press **Home** to fit the view. You'll see a tall box with 24 pins on each side.
4. Click an empty spot **left of the left pins**, in the gap between pin `12` and pin `3V3_2` (about y = −40.6 mm in the readout at the bottom). Drag **down and to the right** past the last pin on the right side, then release. Always drag left-to-right so only pins fully inside the box are selected. The body rectangle should not highlight; 20 pins should.
5. Press **Delete**. Pins `3V3_2, 24…32` and `GND3, 41…33` disappear, leaving 14 per side.
6. Double-click the body rectangle. Set **End Y** to `-40.005` (it was `-65.405`) and click **OK**.
7. Open the **Pin Table** (the table icon in the toolbar). It must list exactly 28 pins: `GND2, 0–12` and `VIN, GND1, 3V3_1, 23–13`.
8. Click **Symbol Properties** (gear icon) and set:
   - Value: `Teensy 4.0`
   - Description: `Teensy 4.0, 28 through-hole contacts`
   - Datasheet: `https://www.pjrc.com/store/teensy40.html`
   - Footprint: `VRITRA_footprints:Teensy 4.0_hdr` (you'll make that footprint next)
9. Press **Ctrl+S**.

## Part 2: Footprint (Footprint Editor)

1. Open the **Footprint Editor**. In the left list open **VRITRA_footprints** and double-click **Teensy 4.1_hdr**.
2. Choose **File → Save a Copy As…**. Pick library **VRITRA_footprints** and name it `Teensy 4.0_hdr`. This keeps editing the 4.1, so next **double-click `Teensy 4.0_hdr` in the left list** to open the copy.
3. Use the grid readout to find the gap between pad `13` and pad `GND3`, at about x = 34.3 mm. Click above and left of that gap (outside the board area, y about −3 mm). Drag **down and to the right** past all the remaining pads, to about x = 62 mm and y = 19 mm, then release. Only the 20 right-hand pads should highlight.
4. Press **Delete**. You should see the top row `VIN, GND1, 3V3_1, 23…13` and the bottom row `GND2, 0…12`.
5. Double-click the large **silkscreen outline rectangle**. Set **End X** to `34.29` (was `59.69`) and click **OK**.
6. Double-click the **courtyard rectangle** (the outermost one). Set **End X** to `34.54` (was `59.94`) and click **OK**.
7. Open **Footprint Properties** and set Value to `Teensy 4.0_hdr`.
8. Run **Inspect → Footprint Checker**. Expect no errors; the courtyard exists and no pad numbers repeat.
9. Press **Ctrl+S**.

Then print it at 100 % scale and lay the real Teensy 4.0 on the paper to check the holes.

**Not included:** the 5-pin end row (`On/Off`, `Program`, `GND`, `3.3V`, `VBat`). Only add those pads if you actually soldered pins into that row.

**Still to do:** you attached the ICM-20948 DXF and STEP. I read the DXF: two rows of six pins at 2.54 mm, rows 12.7 mm apart, four mounting holes, and a 21 × 17 mm outline. I haven't finished the ICM-20948 footprint or the Board 3 wiring yet. Tell me when the 4.0 steps are done and I'll continue with those.

**What's in it**

| Area | Contents |
|---|---|

| Harness | J1 mates Board 1 J5 straight through: GPS data, `WDT_RSTn`, `WDT_PET`, `PARA_TLM`, GND |

| GNSS input | U5 buffer (SN74LVC1G125) on this board's own 3.3 V rail, with a 100 kΩ pull-down |
| Cross-board protection | 330 Ω in series on `WDT_RSTn`, `WDT_PET` and `PARA_TLM` |

The wiring follows the TDK datasheet you shared. The module's chip address pin (AD0) is tied high for 0x69. Its CS pin is tied high for I²C mode, and the FSYNC pin is tied to GND because the datasheet says to if unused.

**The footprint:** `ICM20948_7Semi_hdr12` is now in your shared `VRITRA_footprints.pretty`. I built it from 7Semi's DXF: two rows of six pins at 2.54 mm, rows 12.7 mm apart, four mounting holes, 21 × 17 mm outline. It loads in KiCad with 12 plated pads and 4 holes. Pin 1 is the square pad, and the origin is pad 1 like your other header footprints.

**Things to know**
- **Library setup:** your ICM-20948 symbol is in its own file (`ICM-20948.kicad_sym`), not in `VRITRA_symbols`. I added project library tables for both files, with paths relative to Board 1.
- **DS3231 clock:** it isn't on Board 3, since you didn't ask for it. It would need a cable to Board 1's J7.
- **Files you attached:** the `BO-10167.pdf` is TDK's chip datasheet, not a 7Semi board document. It was still the right source for the pin rules above.
- **Unused TX1:** pin 1 is left unconnected on purpose. Only Board 1 drives the GNSS.

**To verify on the real parts**
1. Footprint pin order: I assumed pins 1–6 are one row left to right and 7–12 the other. Check this against the module's silkscreen.
2. Mounting-hole diameter: I used Ø2.2 mm (M2).
3. CS pin: check the module doesn't already pull it a different way.
4. J1 cable: check it pin 1 to pin 1 against Board 1 J5.

Tell me when you're ready and I'll start on the Board 3 PCB placement, or on the Board 5 schematic once your Teensy 4.0 symbol exists.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T09:46:20)

Board 3 covers 14 of the 16 mandatory fields with sensors and data paths already on it, and I added the one missing piece: a connector for a second DS3231 RTC. The schematic is updated in `Board 3.kicad_sch`, with 0 ERC violations and all 133 pin connections matching the plan.

**What the guidelines say**
- The 16 mandatory fields are on page 18 of the guidelines.
- **Only the transmitted telemetry is graded** (page 19). After a failover, Board 3 must send all 16 fields, or you lose those points.
- The guidelines (6.1 ix and 6.2 ii) require mission time and flight state to survive a processor reset. Your CDR (pages 29 and 75) answers that with a DS3231 RTC with EEPROM.

**Where each field comes from on Board 3**

| Field | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Team ID, 3 Packet count, 15 Software state | Firmware; the count and state need the RTC module's EEPROM to survive a reset | OK with the change below |
| 2 Mission time | DS3231 on the new J4 | Added |
| 4–6 Altitude, pressure, temperature | BMP585 (U3) | OK |
| 7 Bus voltage | INA226 #B on Board 4, via J2 | OK |
| 8–12 GNSS | L89 on Board 1, via J1 pin 1, buffer U5, Teensy pin 0 | OK, shared |
| 13 Acceleration, roll, pitch | ICM-20948 (U2) | OK |
| 14 Gyro spin rate | Board 5 Hall sensor, via J1 pin 4, Teensy pin 28 | OK, shared |
| 16 Optional data | Board 2 sensors, wired to Board 1 only | Not available after a takeover |

**What I changed:** I added connector **J4 "RTC"**, wired exactly like Board 1's J7 (SDA, SCL, 3.3 V, GND). The CDR lists only one DS3231, and that one is on Board 1. If the backup reset after taking over, it would lose its mission clock. **You need to buy a second DS3231 module** (about ₹299). The ICM-20948 stays at address 0x69, because the DS3231 uses 0x68.

**Compared with Board 1 and Board 2**
- J1 mates Board 1 J5 straight through.
- J3 and J2 use the same pin order as Board 1's J1 and J2.
- The radio uses the same locked pins.
- The BMP585 is wired the same way as on Board 1.
- Test points, DNP pull-ups and sheet style follow Board 2.
- I added a note to cut the Teensy VIN–VUSB bridge, as on Board 1.

**Risks outside Board 3**
1. **GNSS (fields 8–12, five of the 16):** Board 3 depends on Board 1's L89 staying powered. A failure of Board 1's 3.3 V rail would take out both Board 1 and the backup's position data. You own a second L89, but it would need its own sky-facing antenna in the stack. I did not add it; tell me if you want that.
2. **Spin rate (field 14):** this needs Board 5 running.
3. **Optional bonus data (field 16):** the environmental sensors are only wired to Board 1. After a takeover, that field is empty, which costs bonus points only. Sharing the Board 2 bus with the backup would add a failure risk, so I'd leave it.
4. **Accelerometer range:** the ICM-20948 reads up to ±16 g, while Board 1's LSM6DSO32 reads ±32 g. It's fine for telemetry, but expect clipping during the 30 g shock events.

The full field-by-field map is also written on the Board 3 sheet, in the Notes box.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - KiCad 10.0.6; `kicad-cli` (sch erc, sch export netlist/svg, pcb drc --schematic-parity, export gerbers/drill/pos, fp export svg). pcbnew Python at `C:/Program Files/KiCad/10.0/bin/python.exe`.
   - `.kicad_sch`/`.kicad_pcb`/`.kicad_pro` text formats. `lib_symbols` cache uses `Nickname:Symbol`. Instances use `(instances (project "<name>" (path "/<root-uuid>")))`.
   - Schematic generator pattern (`build_b2.py`/`build_b3.py`):
     - Pin points read from library symbols via `xf()`: `rx = px*cos + py*sin; ry = px*sin - py*cos; sheet = (sx+rx, sy+ry)` (validated).
     - Stub directions from pin angle; labels, power symbols and no-connect flags at stub ends.
     - ERC-driven layout rules: GND stubs short (n=4), label stubs long (n=8); the +3V3 apex touches a wire 2.54 mm above its pin, so stagger stubs or route short-then-down.
     - PWR_FLAG via a T-junction with staggered stubs.
   - Verification pattern: ERC plus netlist-vs-expected-map script (`verify_b2.py`, `verify_b3.py`).
   - KiCad 10 uses `.history` git folder for autosaves. Library-nickname problem: the global `VRITRA_symbols` entry points at a folder, so project-level lib tables were written (relative `${KIPRJMOD}/../Board 1/...`).
   - Robu capabilities: max drill 6.30 mm so big holes must be Edge.Cuts; min trace/space 0.09; via ring 0.13; PTH ring 0.25; hole-hole 0.5; mask dam 0.20 green / 0.254 other; silk ≥ 0.153 line and 1.0 mm text.
   - Board 1 PCB reference config:
     - Stackup F.Cu / In1.Cu (GND) / In2.Cu (3V3) / B.Cu, 1.6062 mm (prepreg 0.2104, core 1.065, prepreg 0.2104), ENIG, red mask in stackup, tenting both sides.
     - Net classes: Default clearance 0.33, track 0.25, via 0.6/0.3; Power clearance 0.33, track 0.6, via 0.8/0.4, patterns `+5V`, `+3V3`, `GND`.
     - Rules: min_clearance 0.2, copper-edge 0.5, hole clearance 0.25, hole-to-hole 0.5, silk clearance 0.15, text ≥ 1.0 mm / 0.16 stroke, mask-to-copper 0.05, min_via_diameter 0.6, annular 0.15.
     - Outline: Edge.Cuts circle centre (100,100) radius 55, width 0.1.
     - Holes: rods Ø8.5 at (147,100), (100,53), (53,100), (100,147); antenna Ø13 at (100,100).
     - Keepout rule areas are CIRCLES: r 6.5 around each rod and r 8 around the antenna; layers F.Cu/B.Cu/In1/In2; tracks and vias not allowed; copper pour not allowed; pads and footprints allowed; names `KEEPOUT_H1..H5`.
     - Inner planes: zone polygon r = 54.5; `PLANE_GND` on In1.Cu net GND; `PLANE_3V3` on In2.Cu net +3V3 priority 1; connect_pads clearance 0.3; min_thickness 0.25; thermal gap 0.3, spoke 0.5; island_removal_mode 0 (always).
   - Maze router (`maze.py`, 0.1 mm grid, Dijkstra, clearance baked in, rotation-aware pad boxes via `pad.GetBoundingBox()`); GND via fanout approach (`gndstub.py`); snap-via lesson: never blanket-snap endpoints to vias.
   - pcbnew SWIG quirks: `FootprintLoad` via `PCB_IO_MGR.FindPlugin`; `BOARD.Remove()` poisons wrappers (save and reload); get netcode from a pad; `SetDoNotAllowZoneFills`; footprint field API has `GetField/SetField/GetFields`; zone API has `SetIsRuleArea`, `SetDoNotAllow*`, `SetThermalReliefGap`, `SetThermalReliefSpokeWidth`, `SetPadConnection`, `SetIslandRemovalMode`.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T11:07:38)

Your rod is Ø8 and the antenna is Ø13, and the middle circle is the antenna hole. All your boards already use Ø13 for it. They use Ø8.5 for the rod holes: a Ø8 rod can't pass through a Ø8.0 hole, so the extra 0.5 mm is clearance. Board 3 and Board 1 both have this. Board 2's PCB isn't built yet and will use the same sizes. If you want the rod holes to be exactly Ø8.0 instead, say so and I'll change them.

I can't see the Teensy image you sent earlier, so this pin list comes from the standard Teensy 4.0 pinout. Compare it against your image before you trust it.

## Teensy 4.0 facts
- The board is 35.56 × 17.78 mm. The two header rows are 15.24 mm apart at 2.54 mm pitch, with 14 pins per row. The micro-USB end is the top.
- I'm leaving out the bottom SMT pads (the 24–33 group, VBAT, D+/D−, program and on/off). Board 5 only needs the header. If you want them, send a photo of the underside and I'll add them.

## Pin table
Numbering is DIP-style: pads 1–14 run down the left row and 15–28 run up the right row. "Type" is the KiCad electrical type.

| Pad | Name | Alternate functions | Type |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GND | | Power input |
| 2 | 0 | RX1, CRX2, CS1 | Bidirectional |
| 3 | 1 | TX1, CTX2, MISO1 | Bidirectional |
| 4 | 2 | OUT2 | Bidirectional |
| 5 | 3 | LRCLK2 | Bidirectional |
| 6 | 4 | BCLK2 | Bidirectional |
| 7 | 5 | IN2 | Bidirectional |
| 8 | 6 | OUT1D | Bidirectional |
| 9 | 7 | RX2, OUT1A | Bidirectional |
| 10 | 8 | TX2, IN1 | Bidirectional |
| 11 | 9 | OUT1C | Bidirectional |
| 12 | 10 | CS, MQSR | Bidirectional |
| 13 | 11 | MOSI, CTX1 | Bidirectional |
| 14 | 12 | MISO, MQSL | Bidirectional |
| 15 | 13 | SCK, LED | Bidirectional |
| 16 | 14 / A0 | TX3, SPDIF_OUT | Bidirectional |
| 17 | 15 / A1 | RX3, SPDIF_IN | Bidirectional |
| 18 | 16 / A2 | RX4, SCL1 | Bidirectional |
| 19 | 17 / A3 | TX4, SDA1 | Bidirectional |
| 20 | 18 / A4 | SDA | Bidirectional |
| 21 | 19 / A5 | SCL | Bidirectional |
| 22 | 20 / A6 | TX5, LRCLK1 | Bidirectional |
| 23 | 21 / A7 | RX5, BCLK1 | Bidirectional |
| 24 | 22 / A8 | CTX1 | Bidirectional |
| 25 | 23 / A9 | CRX1, MCLK1 | Bidirectional |
| 26 | 3V3 | | Power output |
| 27 | GND | | Power input |
| 28 | VIN | 5 V in | Power input |

All 5 V power connects to VIN. The Teensy 3V3 pin is its own regulator output, so don't tie it to the board's 3V3 rail.

## Symbol, click by click
1. Open KiCad, then the Symbol Editor.
2. Right-click `VRITRA_symbols` in the left list and choose **New Symbol**.
3. Name it `Teensy_4.0`, set Reference to `U`, and leave "Power symbol" and "Unit" at their defaults. Click OK.
4. Press **P** to place a pin, then click once in the canvas. A pin dialog opens.
5. Set the pin name, number and electrical type from the table. Set length to 2.54 mm and name and number text to 1.27 mm. For the left column choose orientation "Right"; for the right column choose "Left".
6. Place pins at x = −12.7, y = 17.78, 15.24, … down to −15.24 for pads 1–14.
7. Place pins at x = +12.7, y = −15.24 up to 17.78 for pads 15–28. Pad 15 is at the bottom and pad 28 at the top.
8. Repeat step 5 for each pin. The pin table editor (grid icon in the toolbar) is quicker for names and types. Set pin 1 and pin 27 to GND, and in KiCad mark pad 28 VIN as Power input.
9. Draw the body with the rectangle tool, from (−10.16, 20.32) to (10.16, −17.78), with a fill of "Background".
10. Open Symbol Properties and set the Footprint field to `VRITRA_footprints:Teensy_4.0_hdr`. Add a Description such as "Teensy 4.0 header pins". Save.

## Footprint, click by click
1. Open the Footprint Editor, right-click `VRITRA_footprints` and choose **New Footprint**. Name it `Teensy_4.0_hdr` and choose "Through hole".
2. Press **P** and place pad 1. Set it to Through-hole, shape Rectangle, 1.7 × 1.7 mm, drill 1.0 mm, at (0, 0). Pad 1 is the origin.
3. Place pads 2–14 as Circle pads, 1.7 mm with 1.0 mm drill, at x = 0 and y = 2.54 × (n−1). The easy route is to copy pad 1 and use Edit → **Create Array** (linear, 14 pads, 2.54 mm vertical step).
4. Make the right row the same way: 14 pads at x = 15.24. Pad 15 is at y = 33.02 and pad 28 is at y = 0. Renumber so the numbers climb as you go up.
5. Draw the silkscreen outline as a rectangle on F.Silkscreen from (−1.27, −1.27) to (16.51, 34.29). That is the board outline: 17.78 × 35.56 mm, with the pad rows 1.27 mm inside each edge.
6. Draw a courtyard rectangle on F.Courtyard from (−1.52, −1.52) to (16.76, 34.54), with a 0.25 mm margin around the outline. If you add a USB overhang, extend the top edge by about 1.5 mm.
7. Add a pin 1 mark (a small triangle or dot) on F.Silkscreen, and "USB" text on F.Fab at the top edge.
8. Add a Reference text `REF**` on F.Silkscreen and a Value text on F.Fab. Keep the silkscreen text at 1.0 mm size with 0.16 mm stroke to pass the fab limits.
9. Save. In the footprint properties, type `Teensy 4.0 header, 2x14 600 mil` as the description.

Send me the finished symbol and footprint and I'll check them against the pad numbers above. After that I'll build the Board 5 schematic.

Your symbol matches the table I gave you for pads 1–28. It also has five extra pins on the bottom, 29–33 (VBat, 3.3V, GND, Program, on/off), so the footprint needs pads 29–33 as well. Otherwise KiCad will report missing pads when you assign the footprint.

## Header pads 1–28
They are through-hole circles, 1.7 mm pad with 1.0 mm drill. Pad 1 is a square 1.7 × 1.7 so it is easy to spot.

| Left row, x = 0 | y | Right row, x = 15.24 | y |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 GND | 0 | 28 VIN | 0 |
| 2 (0) | 2.54 | 27 GND | 2.54 |
| 3 (1) | 5.08 | 26 3V3 | 5.08 |
| 4 (2) | 7.62 | 25 (23) | 7.62 |
| 5 (3) | 10.16 | 24 (22) | 10.16 |
| 6 (4) | 12.70 | 23 (21) | 12.70 |
| 7 (5) | 15.24 | 22 (20) | 15.24 |
| 8 (6) | 17.78 | 21 (19) | 17.78 |

| 10 (8) | 22.86 | 19 (17) | 22.86 |

## What your drawing shows
- **Header rows:** two rows of 14 holes. The rows are 15.24 mm apart and the pitch is 2.54 mm.
- **End pins:** 5 holes at the end away from the USB, on the same 2.54 mm grid.
- **VUSB pad:** one extra hole just inside the second pad of the top row, near the USB end. The back card labels it **VUSB**. That makes 34 holes, which agrees with the "34 locations" in the PJRC spec.
- **Hole and pad size:** the finished hole is 0.965 mm, so use a 1.0 mm drill and a 1.7 mm pad.
- **Pins 24–33 on the underside:** these are SMT pads, 2.794 × 0.889 mm. I'm leaving them out of the footprint; see the underside note below.

## Corrected pin map
- **Top row, left to right:** VIN, GND, 3V3, 23, 22 … 14, 13.
- **Bottom row, left to right:** GND, 0, 1 … 12.
- **End pins, top to bottom (13 side to 12 side):** On/Off, Program, GND, 3.3V, VBat.

## Underside note
Pins 24–33 on the Teensy's underside are SMT pads, in two columns at x ≈ 24.6 and 28.8 and at the five y values −2.54 to −12.70. Keep copper out of that area if the Teensy sits flat on a header. Your Board 1 footprint is the "hdr" style and has no copper there. I read the 24.59 and 4.17 mm spacings off the drawing, so check them if you want those pads in the footprint.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T19:27:23)

The Board 4 schematic is built and installed in your `Board 4` folder, but I haven't started the PCB. I don't know the real pad layout of your power modules, so I can't make their footprints yet.

ERC finds no electrical errors. Its only 7 findings are missing-footprint links for the three module footprints I haven't made yet. A script checking the netlist against the plan matched all 105 pins. Rendered, the layout looks clean (frames, rails, harness). I haven't checked it in KiCad's own schematic viewer.

## What's on the sheet
- **Battery path:** an XT60 input (J1), then a 20 A mini-blade fuse (F1), then an external switch (a screw terminal, J11), then the switched rail VBAT_SW.
- **Protection:** a 470 µF bulk capacitor on VBAT_SW, an SMBJ15A surge suppressor (D3), and a power LED (D1) with an optional external LED header (J14).
- **Monitoring:** INA226 #A (U1) sits in series with the logic branch and reads voltage and current. INA226 #B (U2) has its shunt inputs tied together, so it reads bus voltage only (field 7 on Board 3).
- **Rails:**
  - **+5V:** logic buck U3.
  - **+5V_SERVO:** buck U4, for the MG90S servos.
  - **V_SERVO_A:** buck U5, for the A20BHM servos.
  - **+3V3_A:** buck U6, for Boards 1 and 2.
  - **+3V3_B:** buck U7, for Boards 3 and 5.
- **Outputs:**
  - **Power cables:** J2–J5 carry power to Boards 1, 2, 3 and 5.
  - **Monitor cables:** J6 and J7 carry the INA226 I²C lines to Boards 1 and 3.
  - **Camera power:** J12 and J13.
  - **High-current outputs:** XT30 outputs J8–J10 for servo A, servo B and the ESC.
- **Pin orders:** pin orders on J2–J7 match what Boards 1–3 already expect.
- **Extras:** 8 test points, a "5 V OK" LED (D2), and input and output capacitors at every buck.

## Choices I made that differ from your guide
- **3.3 V modules run from +5V, not the battery.** The 7Semi 3.3 V module is rated 14 V maximum, and a full 3S pack is 12.6 V before ESC spikes. A 5 V input is well inside its 4.2–14 V range.
- **V_SERVO_A is a fixed 5 V.** The A20BHM is rated 4.8–8.4 V and the 7Semi 5 V module can't be adjusted. Torque will be lower than your 8.4 V analysis.
- **I added the fuse on the board,** because your BOM had none.
- **The switch is a screw terminal,** so only 3 XT30 halves go on Board 4 plus 2 on Board 5 (you bought 5).

## Why I stopped before the PCB
- **Module pad layouts are unknown.** The product pages don't give pin or pad positions for the INA226, AP64500 or AP62301 modules. The three new symbols in `VRITRA_symbols` have guessed pin numbers.
- **What I need from you:** a clear top and bottom photo of one INA226 module, one AP64500 and one AP62301. Dimensions or pad positions would also work. Also tell me whether the INA226 has its own I²C pull-ups and what its shunt marking says.
- **Switch rating:** your switch must be rated at least as high as the 20 A fuse.

## Plan for the 2-layer PCB
Two layers is workable: there is no controller and no fast signals. The high-current path (J1, F1, J11, J10) needs copper pours at least 8 mm wide on both layers, joined with stitching vias. A GND pour fills the rest. Keep traces well clear of pads. The Ø8.5 rod holes and Ø13 antenna hole are drawn directly as board cut-outs, as on Board 3.

Send me the module photos and I'll make the footprints, build and route the board, and run DRC.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T19:52:04)

I cut Board 4 from 52 parts to 26. ERC has no electrical errors, and the netlist check matched all 78 pins. The updated schematic is saved over `Board 4/Board 4.kicad_sch`.

**I read "I will put in a 1 on main powerline" as an inline fuse on the 3S pack.** I removed F1 from the board on that basis. If you meant a switch or something else, tell me.

**What I removed:**
- The on-board fuse F1 and the LED header J14.
- The "5 V OK" LED D2 and its resistor R2.
- All 8 test points.
- Every small capacitor, since the buck and INA226 modules carry their own.

**What is left on Board 4:**
- **Connectors:** the XT60 input J1, the switch terminal J11, the harness connectors J2–J7, the camera power connectors J12 and J13, and the XT30 outputs J8–J10.
- **Modules:** INA226 ×2, AP64500 ×3 and AP62301 ×2.
- **Passive parts:** the 470 µF bulk capacitor C1, the two servo reservoirs C7 and C10, the surge suppressor D3, and the power LED D1 with its resistor R1.

## The five-board stack, part by part
Board 1 and Board 2 are read from your Board 1 and Board 2 schematics. Board 3 and Board 4 are the ones I built. Board 5 isn't built yet and comes from the guide.

| Board | Role | Controller | Parts |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1 Primary telemetry** | Flight computer and primary radio | Teensy 4.1 | Core1121-XF (LR1121), L89HA GNSS, BMP585, LSM6DSO32, W25Q64 flash, MAX98357A beacon amp, TPL5010 watchdog, a BAT54W Schottky, a green and a red LED |
| **2 Environmental** | Bonus gas and air sensors | none | SCD41 (CO₂), SEN0290 lightning sensor (Gravity cable), BMV080 particulate sensor, HDC3020 (PH cable), AS7343 spectral sensor, ML8511 UV sensor, MQ-131 ozone sensor |
| **3 Backup telemetry** | Takes over if Board 1 fails | Teensy 4.1 | Core1121-XF (own radio), BMP585, ICM-20948, SN74LVC1G125 GNSS buffer, three 330 Ω series resistors |
| **4 Power** | Rails and monitoring | none | As listed above |
| **5 Parachute** | Altitude trigger, parafoil servos, flywheel | Teensy 4.0 | BMP585, ICM-20948, A3144 Hall sensor, SN74LVC1G125 GNSS buffer; connectors for 2× A20BHM, 2× MG90S and the ESC |

## Where each board gets and sends things
- **Board 1:** its connectors are J1 PWR_IN, J2 INA226_A, J3 and J4 for Board 2, J5 BACKUP to Board 3, J6 PARA to Board 5, J7 RTC, J8 EXPANSION and J9 GNSS_BAT_EXT. The GNSS goes to Board 3 on a shared line through a 1 kΩ series resistor.
- **Board 2:** it has no processor, and its I²C sensors ride on Board 1's second I²C bus. Power comes from Board 4 on its J3.
- **Board 3:** it is powered but silent. When Board 1's watchdog or heartbeat shows a fault, the firmware starts sending on its own radio. It also carries its own RTC connector J4.
- **Board 4:** it feeds Board 1 on J2, Board 2 on J3, Board 3 on J4 and Board 5 on J5. Rail A (+3V3_A) goes to Boards 1 and 2 and rail B (+3V3_B) goes to Boards 3 and 5. It also feeds the two cameras and the ESC.
- **Board 5:** its own BMP585 decides the 600 m deploy, so it doesn't depend on the other boards. It sends the flywheel spin rate (field 14) to Board 1 on PARA_TLM, and it listens to the GNSS from Board 1 for steering only.

All inter-board cables are straight-through, pin 1 to pin 1. My count of 26 Board 4 parts used the same list as above.

The footprints for the INA226 and the buck modules are still missing, so the PCB still waits on your photos. Send a clear top and bottom photo of one INA226 module, one AP64500 and one AP62301, and tell me the switch's current rating and whether you want the same 20 A fuse value.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-04T21:04:19)

Board 5 is built and installed in `C:\Users\kalbh\Documents\KiCad\cansat final pcb\Board 5`. DRC reports 0 violations, 0 unconnected pads and 0 schematic-parity issues, and the schematic ERC is 0. I haven't opened it in KiCad's own editor, only rendered pictures of it.

**What I checked online**
- **A3144 Hall sensor:** the pin order is VCC, GND, OUT, with an open-collector output and a 4.5–24 V supply.
- **Servo plugs:** the MG90S uses the standard 3-pin RC plug. I wasn't able to confirm the A20BHM's plug or wire colours. Check its plug orientation before powering.
- **A20BHM:** it is rated 4.8–8.4 V, so it runs on the 5 V rail. Torque is 7.2 kg·cm at 4.8 V against 11.4 at 8.4 V.
- **Teensy 4.0, BMP585, ICM-20948, SN74LVC1G125:** I reused the pinouts and footprints we'd already verified.

**What's on the schematic**
- **Teensy 4.0:** its own BMP585 and ICM-20948, the SN74LVC1G125 buffer for the GNSS line, and the Hall header with its 10 kΩ pull-up.
- **Headers:** six 3-pin servo and ESC headers, plus a Hall header. The ESC header carries signal and ground only.
- **Dish servo:** header J11 takes PWM from Board 1's J8 pin 2 (GPIO6) through a 2-pin link, J13. It takes 5 V and ground from this board's servo rail.
- **Power in and out:** three screw terminals take 5 V, servo 5 V and 3.3 V from your own regulators. There are four harness outputs, each carrying +5V, +3V3 and GND.

I read "(4) 3V connectors, (4) 5V, (4) GND" as four harness outputs. I can add loose pin headers if you meant separate ones.

**The PCB**
- **Layout:** it is 2-layer, with the Ø8.5 rod holes and Ø13 antenna hole cut straight into the outline. The Teensy sits at the north with its USB facing west. GND is poured on both sides.
- **Track widths:** the servo rail is 2.0 mm, +5V is 1.5 mm, +3V3 is 0.8 mm and signals are 0.3 mm.
- **Clearance:** the router kept every track at least 0.4 mm from pads. The design rule is 0.35 mm, because the SOT-23-5's own pad gap is 0.35 mm.
- **Fab files:** gerbers, drill files and the position file are in `manufacturing/`, with `Board5_Robu_manufacturing.zip` ready to upload.

**Open items**
- **Dish servo timing:** the 20 s open and close at apogee is firmware on Board 1's Teensy 4.1. Nothing on this board does it.
- **Rail inputs:** your regulators must deliver +5V, servo +5V and +3V3 with a common ground. Board 5 has no regulators of its own.
- **Module pin order:** I haven't verified the ICM-20948 and BMP585 pin order against their silkscreen. Check it before ordering.
- **Mask colour:** the colour in the board file is red from Board 1. Choose it when you order.

I removed Board 4 to the Recycle Bin, so you can restore it. Board 2 as a perfboard needs nothing from me.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-05T11:02:54)

I worked through the review, fixed every CAD item it raised that is fixable in CAD, and regenerated the release packages for Boards 1, 3 and 5. On the installed files all three PCBs pass DRC with every rule at error severity: 0 violations, 0 unconnected, 0 schematic-parity issues. All five schematics are ERC-clean. The only ERC notes left are Board 4's 7 "module has no footprint" notes, expected for a perfboard build. These are CAD checks only; the hardware still has to be proven by the bench tests listed below.

**What changed, against the review:**
- **Board 1 package was stale:** I regenerated it from the exact saved board, so the old 12-net front-copper mismatch is gone.
- **No command path to Board 5:** Board 1's J8 pin 6 (Teensy 4.1 pin 29, Serial7 TX) now goes to Board 5's J13 and on to Teensy 4.0 pin 7 through a buffer. J13 is now a 10-way connector wired pin-for-pin with J8; pin 2 still carries the dish-servo PWM. Board 3 can also send commands to Board 5 after a takeover.
- **One shared servo rail:**
  - The steering servos now have their own input (TB4, fed by the third AP64500).
  - The deployment servos stay on TB2.
  - The dish servo hangs off the deployment rail through a resettable fuse, so a dish fault can't take out deployment.
- **Servo feed through one via:** neither rail's main path now has a via; the only servo via left feeds just the fused dish branch. Board 5 now has 112 ground vias tying its two ground pours together.
- **GNSS line exposed to Board 1 faults:** a 1 kΩ (R12) now sits between the GNSS line and Board 1's own Teensy input. If that Teensy dies or its pin sticks low, Boards 3 and 5 still get GNSS.
- **Spin rate (field 14) went through Board 1:** Board 5 now sends it straight to Board 3 on a new 3-wire cable (J14 → J5), so the backup keeps it even if Board 1 is dead.
- **Buffers on the wrong power rail:** every cross-board input on Boards 3 and 5 now goes through a buffer powered by that board's own Teensy, with a pull-down. The watchdog lines into Board 3 now have 4.7 kΩ in series. No board can feed power into another through a signal pin.
- **ICM-20948 straps at 3.3 V:** on Boards 3 and 5, its chip-select and address pins now tie to the module's own 1.8 V pin.
- **Servo outputs undefined during reset:** every servo and ESC output on Board 5 now has 330 Ω in series and 100 kΩ to ground, so they stay low during reset and programming.
- **Board 2:** the UV sensor module now runs from 5 V, so its on-board regulator has headroom.
- **Board 4:** each camera feed now has its own resettable fuse.
- **Board 1 library warnings:** I refreshed the 7 out-of-date symbol copies after checking every pin was identical, so that warning is gone.

**Your specific concerns:**
- **Solder mask:** I compared each exported mask file against the pads. Every opening is a pad, so all tracks, vias and copper pours are covered, and all vias are tented.
- **Vias inside pads:** my check found vias sitting inside small resistor and capacitor pads on Boards 3 and 5, which wick solder when hand-soldering. I re-routed those nets, so none remain.
- **Pad clearance:** routes keep at least 0.4 mm from other copper on Boards 3 and 5, and avoid running right alongside pads where possible.
- **Board 2 → Board 1, GNSS, LoRa and the backup Teensy:** a script traced all 12 inter-board cables pin by pin through the five schematics and every wire lands on the right pin.
  - Board 2's sensors reach Board 1's second I²C bus (pins 24/25) and its analog pins.
  - GNSS reaches both other boards through their buffers.
  - Both radios keep the same Teensy pins as before.
  - The backup Teensy gets all 16 telemetry fields. The only shared dependency left is the single GNSS receiver on Board 1.

**Clean-up:** the project folder now holds only the five boards, their libraries and the handover. The KiCad history folders, the Board 3 4-layer backup, the `.bak` file, Board 2's old PCB and the old zip files are in the Recycle Bin, so you can restore them.

**Still to prove on real hardware** (CAD can't check these):
- **Fit:** the 94 mm rod circle against the real stack.
- **Modules:** pin order of the 7Semi ICM-20948 and BMP585 boards, and the real pin labels on the Board 4 power modules.
- **Current:** servo stall current with everything running at once.
- **Endurance:** the full 2-hour pad wait on the real battery.
- **Firmware:** watchdog takeover, radio on/off, and the dish open–hold 20 s–close sequence.

The handover lists all of these with the order form values, firmware pin maps for all three Teensys, the cable table and a bring-up sequence.

Upload these three files to Robu, one order each, with green solder mask recommended:
- `Board 1\manufacturing\Board1_Robu_gerbers_revB.zip`
- `Board 3\manufacturing\Board3_Robu_gerbers_revB.zip`
- `Board 5\manufacturing\Board5_Robu_gerbers_revB.zip`

Files are in `C:\Users\kalbh\Documents\KiCad\cansat final pcb\`:
- VRITRA_PCB_HANDOVER.md
- Board 1\Board1_FAB_NOTES.md

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-15T21:00:56)

Yes, your order settings will work for Board 1 and Board 3. Two things need your attention: Board 5 isn't in your order, and you should check the rod holes before paying.

The Board 1/3/5 files are the rev E versions, not the rev C set I made earlier. So I re-checked them myself instead of relying on the README in the folder.

**What I checked on the files you're ordering:**

| Check | Board 1 (4-layer) | Board 3 (2-layer) | Board 5 (2-layer) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Zip made from the current KiCad file (same file hash, zip saved 1 min later) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| KiCad design-rule check, with zones refilled | 0 errors / 0 unconnected / 0 schematic mismatches | 0 / 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 / 0 |
| Thinnest track | 0.20 mm | 0.20 mm | 0.20 mm |
| Smallest via (pad / hole) | 0.6 / 0.3 mm | 0.6 / 0.3 mm | 0.6 / 0.3 mm |
| Vias inside solder pads | 0 | 0 | 1 (R36 pad 2, a ground via, harmless) |
| Gerber files in the zip | Top, bottom and both inner copper layers, mask, silkscreen, outline, drill | All layers plus plated and non-plated drill files | All layers plus plated and non-plated drill files |
| Thinnest strip of solder mask between pads (red needs 0.254 mm or more) | ✅ none thinner | ✅ none thinner | ⚠️ one: 0.25 mm between microSD pads 8 and 9 |

**Your order settings:**
- **2 or 4 layers, 110×110 mm, 1.6 mm thick, FR4 TG135, 1 oz copper, quantity 5:** correct.
- **Red solder mask: OK for Board 1 and Board 3.** Earlier I suggested green because red needs a wider mask strip between pads. In rev E every gap on Boards 1 and 3 is at least 0.254 mm, so red is fine.
- **HASL with lead:** fine. It's the easiest finish to hand-solder.
- **Board 1 inner copper:** pick the standard 0.5 oz if the form asks. The design assumes that.

**Two things to sort out before you pay:**
1. **Board 5 isn't in your order.** Its zip is `Board 5/manufacturing_revE/Board5_Robu_revE.zip`. When you order it:
   - **Green mask is safest.** It keeps the mask strip between microSD pads 8 and 9.
   - **If you want red:** the only risk is that the strip between those two pads may be missing. You'd then need care not to bridge them when soldering. The board still works.
2. **Check the rod holes with your actual rods.** This can't be fixed after the boards are made.
   - The four rod holes are 8.5 mm across, for 8.2 mm rods. That leaves only 0.3 mm of play.
   - The rev E README notes Robu's cutting tolerance is ±0.2 mm, so the real play could be as little as 0.1 mm.
   - Measure your rods. If they're tight or slightly oversize, enlarge the holes on all boards before ordering.
   - The 13 mm antenna hole should be checked the same way.

All of this checks the design files only. The boards still need a current-limited first power-up and bench testing once assembled.

Use these to judge **brand and platform reliability**, but make sure the Indian model shares the design before applying the conclusions.

- Wash programmes that actually matter (cotton, quick, heavy, bedsheet, tub clean). Ignore marketing fluff.
- Hard-water handling: scale protection, heater durability, descaling needs.
- Power-cut memory/auto-restart; voltage range; whether a stabilizer is needed.
- Low water-pressure performance.
- Noise and vibration (important for steadiness); weight; anti-vibration design.
- Water and electricity use per load, and BEE rating.
- Dimensions and installation needs (front-load clearance, drain height, tap type).
- Rat/pest protection and the risk of rats chewing pipes and wiring (common in India).
- **Warranty:** comprehensive years, motor years, and what is excluded.
- **Service network in Satara:** authorised centre location/distance, typical response time, owner experiences.
- **Known failure modes** and **estimated 3-year cost of ownership.** Include electricity, water, detergent type (front-load needs low-foam), likely repairs and an optional extended warranty.
- Price: list price, bank offer, effective price, link, seller and stock status.

## 6. Output I want
1. **Verdict on the Bosch** above: buy / don't buy, with reasons.
2. **Best semi-automatic** under the rule: one clear winner, with the reasons it beats the others.
3. **Best fully automatic** under the rule: one clear winner. Say whether front-load or top-load is better for my situation, and why.
4. **At least 8 ranked options**, mixing semi-auto, top-load FA and front-load FA. For each give:
   - model number;
   - direct Amazon/Flipkart in-stock link;
   - list price → card offer → effective price;
   - key strengths, weaknesses and known problems;
   - 3-year cost-of-ownership estimate;
   - Satara service availability;
   - a reliability score with your reasoning.
5. **Full comparison table** of all candidates across every parameter in section 5.
6. **Models to avoid** in this price range, with the evidence (recurring failures, poor service).
7. **Buying checklist:** what to check on delivery and installation; whether to buy the extended warranty; stand, stabilizer and rat-guard advice; low-foam detergent; and the descaling routine for hard water.
8. **Sources:** links for every key fact, with the date you checked prices and stock.

Do not give generic advice. Tie every recommendation to my budget rule, my PIN code, Satara's water and power conditions, and the goal of low maintenance cost over 2–3 years. If no machine fully meets all the rules, say so and show the closest options and the trade-off.
````

What the prompt makes the researcher do:
- It holds the price rule: what you pay after the card discount must be ₹50,000 or less. So it needs to know which cards you have, and that's the most important blank to fill.
- It checks your Bosch first, finds its exact model number, and says buy or don't buy.
- It looks for things that cause repairs in Satara: hard water (bad for heaters), power cuts and low voltage, rats, and how good local service is.
- It uses owner complaints and repair-cost data from India, and reliability ratings from the US, UK, Australia and Germany, but only where the Indian model is built the same way.

**CLAUDE** (2026-10-19T20:15:49)

Schematics complete and verified. Here's where things stand.

**On the revision — you were right to check.** All five boards say rev E. I set the two new boards to **rev F deliberately**, not by mistake: `Board1_Robu_revE.zip` in your folder is the **4-layer** Board 1. If the new 2-layer boards were also called rev E, you could send the wrong file to Robu and get the wrong board back. Rev F makes them impossible to confuse. Boards 2, 3, 4 and 5 stay at rev E — they're untouched.

**Backup first, as you asked:** `BACKUP_ALL5_BOARDS_revE_2026-10-20.zip` (17 MB, 175 files) — verified to contain all five boards' project, schematic and PCB files.

**The split.** Full reasoning in [BOARD1_SPLIT_SPEC.md](Board%201/BOARD1_SPLIT_SPEC.md). The old board packed 7097 mm² of parts into 9144 mm² — 78% full, which is what forced 4 layers. Now:

| | Board 1.1 | Board 1.2 |
|---|---|---|
| Holds | Teensy 4.1, **L89 GNSS**, **Core1121 LoRa**, selector, GNSS buffers, watchdog, LEDs | BMP585, LSM6DSO32 IMU, W25Q64 flash, MAX98357 amp + speaker, DS3231 RTC |
| Fill | ~59% | ~38% |
| Cables to other boards | J1, J3, J4, J5, J6, J8, J9, J10, J11 — all unchanged | J2, J7 — same pinouts, just plug in here |

Four straight-through JST EH cables join them: **J30↔J40** power (5), **J31↔J41** SPI (8), **J32↔J42** I²C (3), **J33↔J43** audio (5). Board 1.2 also gets five extra JST seats (J44–J48) so you can re-cable any sensor to the Teensy however you like.

**Verified, not assumed:**
- Both schematics: **ERC 0 violations**
- A net-by-net proof that 1.1 + 1.2 joined by the cables is **electrically identical to the old Board 1** — 56 nets, 203 pins, all intact, nothing shorted, nothing dangling
- Boards 2, 3, 4, 5: not modified

**One thing I found along the way that's worth telling you.** The schematic generator from the rev C work had a bug: it rotated symbols the wrong way at 90° and 270°, silently swapping pin 1 and pin 2. Harmless on a resistor — but it reverses a diode or LED. It had in fact reversed the LEDs and the GNSS backup diode on my first pass. I fixed the transform, verified it against KiCad's own netlist at all four rotations, and then **audited all five rev E boards** for the same fault: every electrolytic has its minus to GND, both LEDs have cathode to GND, the BAT54W blocks charging correctly, and the SMBJ15A TVS is the right way round. **Your ordered boards are clean** — nothing to redo there.

**Still to do:** the two PCB layouts — placement, routing, ground pours, DRC, then Robu gerbers. That's the long part, and I'm starting it now. The schematics are already readable if you want to look: [Board1_1_schematic.pdf](Board%201/Board%201.1/Board1_1_schematic.pdf) and [Board1_2_schematic.pdf](Board%201/Board%201.2/Board1_2_schematic.pdf).

One flag before I commit to the layout: **the stack now has six boards instead of five.** More height, more mass, four more cables, on a mass-limited CanSat. If that breaks your mass or height budget, say so now — it's much cheaper to stop here than after routing.

## Done and verified

**Backup** — `BACKUP_ALL5_BOARDS_revE_2026-10-20.zip` (17 MB, 175 files), verified to contain all five boards' project, schematic and PCB files.

**Both schematics are finished and proven correct:**
- `Board 1/Board 1.1/Board1_1.kicad_sch` — Teensy 4.1, L89 GNSS, Core1121 LoRa, selector, GNSS buffers, watchdog, LEDs
- `Board 1/Board 1.2/Board1_2.kicad_sch` — BMP585, LSM6DSO32, W25Q64, MAX98357 + speaker, DS3231, INA226 tap
- **ERC 0 violations on both**
- A net-by-net proof that 1.1 + 1.2 joined by the cables is **electrically identical to the old Board 1**: 56 nets, 203 pins, nothing broken, shorted or dangling, diode and LED polarity included
- A per-sensor trace table confirming **every** Board 1.2 sensor pin reaches the Teensy on a named cable pin
- Boards 2, 3, 4, 5 untouched

**Link: 4 straight-through JST EH cables**, 22 conductors — J30↔J40 power 6-pin (+3V3, **+5V**, +5V_LOGGER, +3V3_LOGGER, GND, GND), J31↔J41 SPI 8-pin, J32↔J42 I²C 3-pin, J33↔J43 audio 5-pin.

**Board 1.1 PCB** placed and fully autorouted once already (**0 unrouted, 0 DRC violations, 0 unconnected, silkscreen clean, narrowest mask web 0.35 mm** — fine for red or green). The job running now is a rebuild with corrected net classes.

## Two things you must know

**1. I could not fit the spare sensor seats on Board 1.1.** Its outer ring is full with the MCU, GNSS, radio and nine harness cables, and I reserved the bands above and below the centre hole as routing corridors — a through-hole connector there walls off both copper layers at once. **All spare 5V/3V3/GND and I²C seats are on Board 1.2** (J44–J51, eight of them). That board is only ~40% full and is the sensor board, so it's the right place, but it's not what you asked for and you should decide if that's acceptable.

**2. I found a real bug in the schematic generator** from the earlier rev C work: it rotated symbols the wrong way at 90°/270°, swapping pin 1 and 2 — harmless on a resistor, **a reversed diode or LED anywhere else**. It had already reversed the LEDs and the GNSS backup diode on my first pass. Fixed and verified against KiCad's own netlist at all four rotations. I then audited **all five rev E boards**: every electrolytic has minus to GND, both LEDs have cathode to GND, the BAT54W blocks charging, the SMBJ15A TVS is correct. **Your ordered boards are clean.**

## Left to do
- Finish/redo the Board 1.1 route with the corrected net classes (0.25 mm clearance, 0.6 mm power traces)
- Build and route Board 1.2 — spec is written (`spec12.json`), modules clustered around the centre hole to leave the ring free for its 14 connectors
- Gerbers for both, and a handover doc

Also note: **the stack is now six boards instead of five** — more height, mass and four more cables. Worth re-checking your CanSat mass budget before fabricating.

Two BOM changes I made to fit 2 layers, both values already in your BOM: Board 1.1 C1 is now a 1206 10 µF ceramic (was a Ø10 mm electrolytic) and C3 a 0805 100 nF (was a disc).

## The etching answer: it won't work, and I can prove why

I tested it properly rather than guessing. Full evidence in [ETCH_FEASIBILITY.md](ETCH_FEASIBILITY.md). Four independent reasons:

**1. It doesn't route.** Same placement, same netlist, only the rules changed:

| Board | rules | unrouted | violations |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| 1.1 | fabricated (0.25 mm) | **0** | **0** |
| 1.1 | etch 0.45/0.50 mm | 67 | 18 |
| 1.1 | etch 0.40/0.40 mm | 46 | 10 |
| 1.2 | etch, bottom-layer only | 30 | 16 |

Board 1.2 is the *sparsest* board in the stack and it still fails. Boards 3 and 5 are denser (2.67 and 2.79 pads/cm² against Board 1.1's 2.16), so they fail harder.

**2. No plated holes is the real killer, and no trace width fixes it.** A pad only connects to the side you can reach with an iron. Modules on female headers and JST connectors are **bottom-solder-only** — 15 such parts on Board 1.1. The router put **274 of 599 segments on the top layer** and left **51 pads reachable only from the top**. On a real etched board those 51 connections wouldn't exist. Seven wire-link vias also landed under component bodies.

**3. Parts finer than etching resolves.** Board 5's microSD socket has a **0.25 mm** pad gap; thirteen SOT-23 packages are at 0.35 mm. With no soldermask there's no dam to stop bridges.

**4. You'd be hand-soldering 80–120 wire links per board.**

The realistic alternative, if home etching is essential, is re-partitioning across 3–4× more boards and swapping to DIP parts (74HC125 DIP-14 replaces four SOT-23 buffers; 74HC157 DIP-16; a microSD breakout module). That's a redesign, not a re-route.

## What I did instead — finished the fabricated boards

| | Board 1.1 | Board 1.2 |
|---|---|---|
| Schematic ERC | **0** | **0** |
| Board DRC | **0 errors** | **0 errors** |
| Unconnected pins | **0** | **0** |
| Mask web (narrowest) | 0.35 mm | 0.80 mm — red *or* green both fine |

- **Split equivalence proven**: 56 nets, 203 pins — Board 1.1 + 1.2 is electrically identical to the old Board 1, polarity included.
- **Your stack check, which you asked for first**: every inter-board cable verified pin by pin across all six boards — **24 cables, 94 pins, 0 mismatches**.
- Gerbers, drills, drill maps, top/bottom assembly PDFs and pick-and-place are in each board's `manufacturing/`.

Getting there took real fixes, not just re-runs: GND was being routed as tracks and colliding with power traces (now left to the pours), 149 degenerate fragments came in from the SES import, and U10's ground pin and U12 were boxed in — I ripped up and re-routed the offending power net to free them.

## Three things to decide

1. **The stack is now six boards, not five.** More height and mass on a mass-limited CanSat — check your budget.
2. **SPI crosses a cable now.** Damping resistors and interleaved grounds are in, but **cap SPI at 10 MHz**, 8 MHz for the off-board IMU and flash.
3. **Spare 5V/3V3/GND seats are all on Board 1.2**, not 1.1 — 1.1's ring is genuinely full. Eight seats, all reaching the primary Teensy.

**A bug worth knowing about:** the rev C schematic generator rotated symbols the wrong way at 90°/270°, swapping pin 1 and 2 — harmless on a resistor, but it reverses a diode or LED, and it *had* reversed your status LEDs and GNSS backup diode on my first pass. Fixed and verified at all four rotations. I then audited all five rev E boards: **the boards you already ordered are clean.**

Full details in [HANDOVER_revF.md](HANDOVER_revF.md). Backup from before any of this: `BACKUP_ALL5_BOARDS_revE_2026-10-20.zip`. Boards 2–5 untouched; the empty etch folders went to the Recycle Bin.

## The guide

[VRITRA_COMPLETE_GUIDE.md](VRITRA_COMPLETE_GUIDE.md) — 36 KB, ten parts, built from the rev E design files plus everything established across our whole conversation:

1. **The mission** — flight profile, the target coordinate, and the rules table showing *which rule forced which design decision* (no LiPo → 21700 pack; mass limit → Ø110 discs; locked radio row → 867.00/865.75 MHz, SF10, sync 0x98)
2. **Architecture** — the five boards, why Board 1 is four layers (measured 78 % fill), the full power tree, and how failover actually works signal by signal
3. **Each board** — components, complete Teensy pin maps, every connector pinout
4. **The harness** — all 24 cables, pin by pin
5. **Mechanical** — the hole pattern, with the unresolved rod-clearance warning
6. **Manufacturing** — what to send, order settings, and the verified numbers
7. **Firmware contract** — what the hardware guarantees and what you must write
8. **Assembly, bring-up order, and the six open risks**
9. **The polarity bug** found and fixed, plus the audit clearing all five boards
10. **File map**

Three things in there I'd particularly point you back to:

- **Never fly without Board 3** — the entire avionics 5 V runs through its INA260 loop. A bypass plug on Board 4 J14 (pin 1 to pin 4) is the workaround.
- **Mask colour**: green is safe everywhere. Red works on Boards 1 and 3, but on Board 5 the 0.25 mm dam between the two microSD pads may not print — a soldering risk, not a board fault.
- **The A20CLM servos** are 10.2 kg·cm, not 35, and two of them share one 5 A buck. That's still unmeasured and it's the most likely thing to bite you.

I also kept the findings from the two rejected routes (the Board 1 split, and home etching) as a short section, so the measurements aren't lost even though the files are gone.
